It was, however, argued that the direct utility of the THF model is dependent on a separation of scales between the
f
-
c
hybridization (
\gamma
) and the onsite Hubbard repulsion of the
f
-electrons (
U_{1}
)
[
35
]
, where the magnitude of the latter should be larger than the former. In this case, a
real-space
description of TBG is possible, in which the
f
-electrons form local moments
[
33
,
273
,
274
,
45
,
276
,
277
,
50
,
275
,
49
,
278
,
171
]
.

Conversely, when
\absolutevalue{\gamma}>U_{1}
, a real-space description of the interaction terms is no longer useful, as the large
f
-
c
hybridization does not allow the polarization of the
f
-fermions to minimize the onsite Hubbard repulsion
H_{U_{1}}
. Because in the
\absolutevalue{\gamma}>U_{1}
limit, the energy scale of the interaction is smaller than the gap between the active and remote TBG, a flat-band-projected approach constitutes a more realistic formulation of the problem
[
282
,
23
]
. Such a description involves the so-called TBG form factors – overlaps between the active TBG band wave functions at different momenta
[
269
]
. The TBG form factors can easily be computed numerically, but in order to obtain them analytically, the expressions of the active TBG wave functions are necessary. Obtaining the latter is an impossible task in the original BM model with a large number of bands, but becomes tractable within the THF formalism.

In this appendix, we show that in the
\absolutevalue{\gamma}>U_{1}
limit, the single-particle THF model can still be employed to derive analytical expressions for the TBG flat-band wave functions. We then show how the latter can be used to compute the TBG form factors analytically. In turn, this enables us to analytically obtain the charge-one excitations of TBG using the method introduced by Ref.
[
24
]
. Finally, we also show that for
\absolutevalue{\gamma}>U_{1}
, a flat-band projected limit of the THF model can be defined, wherein the charge-one excitations of TBG around charge neutrality can also be obtained analytically. This confirms the conceptual utility of the THF model not just in the
\absolutevalue{\gamma}<U_{1}
limit, but also in the opposite limit
\absolutevalue{\gamma}>U_{1}
.

B.1
Flat-band wave functions

In the THF model, the wave functions of the flat bands can be obtained analytically within the zero-bandwidth limit, which is defined when
v_{\star}^{\prime}=v_{\star}^{\prime\prime}=0
, and
M=0

[
33
]
. In this limit, the model exhibits an additional symmetry, which Ref.
[
33
]
dubs “chiral symmetry”. Notably, the chiral symmetry of the THF model within the zero-bandwidth limit is
different
from the chiral sublattice symmetry of the TBG BM model in the
w_{0}/w_{1}=0
limit
[
61
]
. This can be seen by noting that in the
w_{0}/w_{1}=0
limit, the active TBG Chern bands are eigenstates of the chiral sublattice operator and are, therefore, graphene sublattice-polarized. However, the active TBG Chern bands, obtained analytically from the THF model in its zero-bandwidth limit
[
33
]
are not polarized on the single-layer graphene sublattices. As a result, the analytical expression for the TBG flat bands obtained with the THF model in zero-bandwidth limit are still a good approximation of the realistic TBG bands.

B.1.1
Analytical expression for the TBG flat-band wave functions

Working in the zero-bandwidth limit, the single-particle Hamiltonian from
Eq.

62
can be diagonalized analytically. The fermion operators
\hat{d}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},\zeta,\eta,s}
(
\zeta=\pm
) and
\hat{b}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},j,\eta,s}
(
j=\pm 1,\pm 2
), corresponding to the Chern band eigenstates of the active bands introduced in
Eq.

76
and to the remote-band eigenstates, respectively, are given in the THF model basis by

\displaystyle\hat{d}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},+,\eta,s}

\displaystyle=g_{\mathbf{k}}e^{i\eta\theta_{\mathbf{k}}}\hat{f}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},1,\eta,s}-\eta h_{\mathbf{k}}\hat{c}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},3,\eta,s},

\displaystyle\hat{b}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},+1,\eta,s}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(h_{\mathbf{k}}\hat{f}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},1,\eta,s}-\hat{c}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},1,\eta,s}+\eta g_{\mathbf{k}}e^{-i\eta\theta_{\mathbf{k}}}\hat{c}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},3,\eta,s}\right),

\displaystyle\hat{b}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},+2,\eta,s}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(h_{\mathbf{k}}\hat{f}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},1,\eta,s}+\hat{c}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},1,\eta,s}+\eta g_{\mathbf{k}}e^{-i\eta\theta_{\mathbf{k}}}\hat{c}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},3,\eta,s}\right),

\displaystyle\hat{d}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},-,\eta,s}

\displaystyle=g_{\mathbf{k}}e^{-i\eta\theta_{\mathbf{k}}}\hat{f}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},2,\eta,s}-\eta h_{\mathbf{k}}\hat{c}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},4,\eta,s},

\displaystyle\hat{b}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},-1,\eta,s}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(h_{\mathbf{k}}\hat{f}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},2,\eta,s}-\hat{c}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},2,\eta,s}+\eta g_{\mathbf{k}}e^{i\eta\theta_{\mathbf{k}}}\hat{c}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},4,\eta,s}\right),

\displaystyle\hat{b}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},-2,\eta,s}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(h_{\mathbf{k}}\hat{f}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},2,\eta,s}+\hat{c}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},2,\eta,s}+\eta g_{\mathbf{k}}e^{i\eta\theta_{\mathbf{k}}}\hat{c}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},4,\eta,s}\right),

(79)

where

\displaystyle g_{\mathbf{k}}=\frac{v_{\star}\absolutevalue{\vk}}{\sqrt{\absolutevalue{v_\star\vk}^{2}+\gamma^{2}e^{-|\mathbf{k}|^{2}\lambda^{2}}}},\quad h_{\mathbf{k}}=\frac{\gamma e^{-|\mathbf{k}|^{2}\lambda^{2}/2}}{\sqrt{\absolutevalue{v_\star\vk}^{2}+\gamma^{2}e^{-|\mathbf{k}|^{2}\lambda^{2}}}},\quad\mbox{and}\quad e^{i\theta_{\mathbf{k}}}=\frac{k_{x}+ik_{y}}{\absolutevalue{\vk}}.

(80)

In the resulting band-basis, the single-particle Hamiltonian can then be written simply as

H_{0}^{\text{TBG}}=\sum_{\mathbf{k},j,\eta,s}\sqrt{\gamma^{2}e^{-\absolutevalue{\vk}^{2}\lambda^{2}}+v_{\star}^{2}\absolutevalue{\vk}^{2}}\left(2\absolutevalue{j}-3\right)\hat{b}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},j,\eta,s}\hat{b}_{\mathbf{k},j,\eta,s}.

(81)

For simplicity, in
Eq.

79
, we restricted the
c
-electrons’ momenta to the first moiré BZ. In the generic case that includes all the
c
-electrons with momenta
\absolutevalue{\vk}<\Lambda_{c}
, the
\hat{d}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},\zeta,\eta,s}
operators are given by

\displaystyle\hat{d}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},+,\eta,s}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{k}}}}\hat{f}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},1,\eta,s}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{k}}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{G}\\
|\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{G}|<\Lambda_{c}\end{subarray}}\frac{\gamma e^{-\frac{1}{2}\lambda^{2}\absolutevalue{\vk+\vG}^{2}}/v_{\star}}{\eta(k_{x}+G_{x})+i(k_{y}+G_{y})}\hat{c}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{G},3,\eta,s},

(82)

\displaystyle\hat{d}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},-,\eta,s}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{k}}}}\hat{f}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},2,\eta,s}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{k}}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{G}\\
|\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{G}|<\Lambda_{c}\end{subarray}}\frac{\gamma e^{-\frac{1}{2}\lambda^{2}\absolutevalue{\vk+\vG}^{2}}/v_{\star}}{\eta(k_{x}+G_{x})-i(k_{y}+G_{y})}\hat{c}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{G},4,\eta,s},

(83)

where the normalization prefactor reads as

\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{k}}=1+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{G}\\
|\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{G}|<\Lambda_{c}\end{subarray}}\frac{\gamma^{2}e^{-\lambda^{2}\absolutevalue{\vk+\vG}^{2}}/v_{\star}^{2}}{|\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{G}|^{2}}\ .

(84)

We remind the reader that the operator
d_{\mathbf{k},\zeta,\eta,s}^{\dagger}
corresponds to a Chern band with Chern number
\zeta\cdot\eta
. It is worth noting that, for the valley
\eta=+
, the
\hat{d}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},\zeta,\eta,s}
operator is the same as the Chern-band operator introduced by Ref.
[
269
]
. However, in valley
\eta=-
, the
\hat{d}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},\zeta,-,s}
operator introduced here corresponds to the
\hat{d}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},-\zeta,-,s}
one of Ref.
[
269
]
.

The wave function of the TBG flat bands can, therefore, be expressed
analytically
in terms of the
f
- and
c
-electron operators. The latter also admit analytical expressions given by
Eqs.

48
,
49
,
55
,
56
,
57
and
58
. In turn, this enables one to obtain the active band TBG wave functions analytically within the plane wave basis from
Eq.

35
, a result which is not otherwise directly available from the BM model.

By combining
Eqs.

45
,
53
and
79
, we can express the electron operators of the flat TBG Chern bands in terms of the the low-energy TBG operators from
Eq.

35
as

\displaystyle\hat{d}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},+,\eta,s}=g_{\mathbf{k}}e^{i\eta\theta_{\mathbf{k}}}\sum_{\mathbf{Q},\beta}v^{\eta}_{\mathbf{Q}\beta;1}(\mathbf{k})\hat{c}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q},\beta,\eta,s}-\eta h_{\mathbf{k}}\sum_{\mathbf{Q},\beta}\tilde{u}_{\mathbf{Q}\beta;3}^{\eta}(\mathbf{0})\hat{c}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q},\beta,\eta,s},

\displaystyle\hat{d}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},-,\eta,s}=g_{\mathbf{k}}e^{-i\eta\theta_{\mathbf{k}}}\sum_{\mathbf{Q},\beta}v^{\eta}_{\mathbf{Q}\beta;2}(\mathbf{k})\hat{c}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q},\beta,\eta,s}-\eta h_{\mathbf{k}}\sum_{\mathbf{Q},\beta}\tilde{u}_{\mathbf{Q}\beta;4}^{\eta}(\mathbf{0})\hat{c}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{Q},\beta,\eta,s}.

(85)

Comparing with
Eq.

76
, we can directly identify the Chern band wave functions

U_{\mathbf{Q}\beta;\eta}^{\zeta}(\mathbf{k})=e^{i\eta\zeta\theta_{\mathbf{k}}}g_{\mathbf{k}}v^{\eta}_{\mathbf{Q}\beta;\zeta}(\mathbf{k})-\eta h_{\mathbf{k}}\tilde{u}^{\eta}_{\mathbf{Q}\eta;i_{\zeta}}(\mathbf{0}),

(86)

where, for simplicity, we have denoted
i_{\zeta=+}=3
,
i_{\zeta=-}=4
.

B.1.2
Analytical expressions for the TBG form factors

Another useful quantity which can be obtained analytically using
Eq.

86
are the TBG form factors corresponding to the active bands. The latter arise when projecting the TBG density operator into the Chern-band basis. Specifically, the projected TBG density operator is given by

\delta\rho\left(\mathbf{q}\right)=\sum_{\mathbf{k},\eta,s}\sum_{\zeta,\zeta^{\prime}}M_{\zeta\zeta^{\prime}}^{(\eta)}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q})\left(\hat{d}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q},\zeta,\eta,s}\hat{d}_{\mathbf{k},\zeta^{\prime},\eta,s}-\frac{1}{2}\delta_{\mathbf{q},\mathbf{0}}\right),

(87)

where the corresponding form factors are defined by

M_{\zeta\zeta^{\prime}}^{(\eta)}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime})=\sum_{\mathbf{Q},\beta}U^{*\zeta}_{\mathbf{Q}\beta;\eta}(\mathbf{k})U^{\zeta^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{Q}\beta;\eta}(\mathbf{k}^{\prime}).

(88)

The form factors can be calculated via
Eq.

86
. Approximately, we find that

\displaystyle M_{\zeta\zeta^{\prime}}^{(\eta)}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime})\approx\sum_{\mathbf{Q},\beta}\left(g_{\mathbf{k}}g_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}e^{-i\theta_{\mathbf{k}}\zeta-i\theta_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}\zeta^{\prime}}v^{*\eta}_{\mathbf{Q}\beta;\zeta}(\mathbf{k})v^{\eta}_{\mathbf{Q}\beta;\zeta^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k}^{\prime})+h_{\mathbf{k}}h_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}\tilde{u}^{*\eta}_{\mathbf{Q}\beta;i_{\zeta}}(\mathbf{0})\tilde{u}^{\eta}_{\mathbf{Q}\beta;i_{\zeta^{\prime}}}(\mathbf{0})\right),

(89)

where we have dropped the cross-terms
v^{*\eta}_{\mathbf{Q}\beta;\zeta}(\mathbf{k})\tilde{u}^{\eta}_{\mathbf{Q}\eta;i_{\zeta^{\prime}}}(\mathbf{0})
and
v^{\eta}_{\mathbf{Q}\beta;\zeta}(\mathbf{k}^{\prime})\tilde{u}^{*\eta}_{\mathbf{Q}\eta;i_{\zeta^{\prime}}}(\mathbf{0})
. In general, the
f
- and
c
-fermions wave functions are orthogonal at any momentum
\mathbf{k}
in the moiré BZ

\sum_{\mathbf{Q}\beta}v^{*\eta}_{\mathbf{Q}\beta;\zeta}(\mathbf{k})\tilde{u}^{\eta}_{\mathbf{Q}\beta;i_{\zeta}}(\mathbf{k})=0\,,

(90)

Coupled with the approximation
\tilde{u}_{\mathbf{Q}\beta,i}(\mathbf{k})\approx\tilde{u}_{\mathbf{Q}\beta,i}(\mathbf{0})
from
Eq.

54
, this implies that the cross-terms should approximately vanish. Using also the orthonormal condition for the
c
-electron wave functions at the
\Gamma_{M}
point,
\sum_{\mathbf{Q},\beta}\tilde{u}^{*\eta}_{\mathbf{Q}\beta;i_{\zeta}}(\mathbf{0})\tilde{u}^{\eta}_{\mathbf{Q}\beta;i_{\zeta^{\prime}}}(\mathbf{0})=\delta_{\zeta,\zeta^{\prime}}
, we find that

M_{\zeta\zeta^{\prime}}^{(\eta)}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime})\approx\sum_{\mathbf{Q},\beta}\left(g_{\mathbf{k}}g_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}e^{-i(\theta_{\mathbf{k}}-\theta_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}})\zeta}v^{*\eta}_{\mathbf{Q}\beta;\zeta}(\mathbf{k})v^{\eta}_{\mathbf{Q}\beta;\zeta^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k}^{\prime})+h_{\mathbf{k}}h_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}\delta_{\zeta,\zeta^{\prime}}\right).

(91)

To further simplify the expression from
Eq.

91
, we now compute the sum
\sum_{\mathbf{Q},\beta}v^{\eta}_{\mathbf{Q}\beta;\zeta}(\mathbf{k})v^{*\eta}_{\mathbf{Q}\beta;\zeta^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k}^{\prime})
using the analytical formulae for the
f
-electron wave functions from
Eqs.

50
and
51
. Additionally, we approximate the summation over
\mathbf{Q}
by an integral according to

\sum_{\mathbf{Q}}\rightarrow\frac{\Omega_{0}}{2\pi^{2}}\int\text{d}^{2}Q.

(92)

This allows us to obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{Q},\beta}v^{\eta}_{\mathbf{Q}\beta;+}(\mathbf{k})v^{*\eta}_{\mathbf{Q}\beta;+}(\mathbf{k}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\frac{\Omega_{0}}{2\pi^{2}}\frac{2\pi}{\Omega_{0}\sqrt{\mathcal{N}_{f,\mathbf{k}}\mathcal{N}_{f,\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}}}\int\text{d}^{2}Q\left\{\lambda_{1}^{2}\alpha_{1}^{2}e^{-(\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{Q})^{2}\lambda_{1}^{2}/2-(\mathbf{k}^{\prime}-\mathbf{Q})^{2}\lambda_{1}^{2}/2}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\lambda_{2}^{4}\alpha_{2}^{2}e^{-(\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{Q})^{2}\lambda_{2}^{2}/2-(\mathbf{k}^{\prime}-\mathbf{Q})^{2}\lambda_{2}^{2}/2}\left[i\eta(k_{x}-Q_{x})-(k_{y}-Q_{y})\right]\left[-i\eta(k_{x}^{\prime}-Q_{x})-(k_{y}^{\prime}-Q_{y})\right]\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathcal{N}_{f,\mathbf{k}}\mathcal{N}_{f,\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}}}\left[\alpha_{1}^{2}e^{-\frac{\absolutevalue{\vk_1-\vk_2}^{2}\lambda_{1}^{2}}{4}}+\alpha_{2}^{2}\left(1-\frac{\absolutevalue{\vk_1-\vk_2}^{2}\lambda_{1}^{2}}{4}\right)e^{-\frac{\absolutevalue{\vk_1-\vk_2}^{2}\lambda_{2}^{2}}{4}}\right],

(93)

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{Q},\beta}v^{\eta}_{\mathbf{Q}\beta;-}(\mathbf{k})v^{*\eta}_{\mathbf{Q}\beta;-}(\mathbf{k}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\frac{\Omega_{0}}{2\pi^{2}}\frac{2\pi}{\Omega_{0}\sqrt{\mathcal{N}_{f,\mathbf{k}}\mathcal{N}_{f,\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}}}\int\text{d}^{2}Q\left\{\lambda_{1}^{2}\alpha_{1}^{2}e^{-(\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{Q})^{2}\lambda_{1}^{2}/2-(\mathbf{k}^{\prime}-\mathbf{Q})^{2}\lambda_{1}^{2}/2}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\lambda_{2}^{4}\alpha_{2}^{2}e^{-(\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{Q})^{2}\lambda_{1}^{2}/2-(\mathbf{k}^{\prime}-\mathbf{Q})^{2}\lambda_{1}^{2}/2}\left[-i\eta(k_{x}-Q_{x})-(k_{y}-Q_{y})\right]\left[i\eta(k_{x}^{\prime}-Q_{x})-(k_{y}^{\prime}-Q_{y})\right]\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathcal{N}_{f,\mathbf{k}}\mathcal{N}_{f,\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}}}\left[\alpha_{1}^{2}e^{-\frac{\absolutevalue{\vk_1-\vk_2}^{2}\lambda_{1}^{2}}{4}}+\alpha_{2}^{2}\left(1-\frac{\absolutevalue{\vk_1-\vk_2}^{2}\lambda_{1}^{2}}{4}\right)e^{-\frac{\absolutevalue{\vk_1-\vk_2}^{2}\lambda_{2}^{2}}{4}}\right],

(94)

and

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{Q},\beta}v^{\eta}_{\mathbf{Q}\beta;+}(\mathbf{k})v^{*\eta}_{\mathbf{Q}\beta;-}(\mathbf{k}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\frac{\Omega_{0}}{2\pi^{2}}\frac{2\pi}{\Omega_{0}\sqrt{\mathcal{N}_{f,\mathbf{k}}\mathcal{N}_{f,\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}}}\int\text{d}^{2}Q\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2}^{2}\left[-\eta\left(k_{x}^{\prime}-Q_{x}\right)-i\left(k_{y}^{\prime}-Q_{y}^{\prime}\right)\right]e^{-\frac{1}{2}(\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{Q})^{2}\lambda_{1}^{2}-\frac{1}{2}(\mathbf{k}^{\prime}-\mathbf{Q})^{2}\lambda_{2}^{2}}

\displaystyle+\frac{\Omega_{0}}{2\pi^{2}}\frac{2\pi}{\Omega_{0}\sqrt{\mathcal{N}_{f,\mathbf{k}}\mathcal{N}_{f,\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}}}\int\text{d}^{2}Q\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2}^{2}\left[-\eta(k_{x}-Q_{x})-i(k_{y}-Q_{y})\right]e^{-\frac{1}{2}(\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{Q})^{2}\lambda_{2}^{2}-\frac{1}{2}(\mathbf{k}^{\prime}-\mathbf{Q})^{2}\lambda_{1}^{2}}=0,

(95)

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{Q},\beta}v^{\eta}_{\mathbf{Q}\beta;-}(\mathbf{k})v^{*\eta}_{\mathbf{Q}\beta;+}(\mathbf{k}^{\prime})=0.

(96)

Moreover, the continuum limit from
Eq.

92
enables us to approximate the normalization constant from
Eq.

52
and obtain

\mathcal{N}_{f,\mathbf{k}}\approx\frac{\Omega_{0}}{2\pi^{2}}\int\text{d}^{2}Q\left[\alpha_{1}^{2}\frac{2\pi\lambda_{1}^{2}}{\Omega_{0}}e^{-(\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{Q})^{2}\lambda_{1}^{2}}+\alpha_{2}^{2}\frac{2\pi\lambda_{2}^{4}}{\Omega_{0}}(\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{Q})^{2}e^{-(\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{Q})^{2}\lambda_{2}^{2}}\right]=1.

(97)

As a result, we find that

\sum_{\mathbf{Q},\beta}v^{\eta}_{\mathbf{Q}\beta;\zeta}(\mathbf{k})v^{*\eta}_{\mathbf{Q}\beta;\zeta^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k}^{\prime})\approx\delta_{\zeta,\zeta^{\prime}}\frac{1}{\alpha_{1}^{2}+\alpha_{2}^{2}}\left[\alpha_{1}^{2}e^{-\frac{\absolutevalue{\vk-\vk'}^{2}\lambda_{1}^{2}}{4}}+\alpha_{2}^{2}\left(1-\frac{\absolutevalue{\vk-\vk'}^{2}\lambda_{1}^{2}}{4}\right)e^{-\frac{\absolutevalue{\vk-\vk'}^{2}\lambda_{2}^{2}}{4}}\right].

(98)

From
Eq.

91
and
Eq.

98
, the TBG form factors can then be written as

M_{\zeta\zeta^{\prime}}^{(\eta)}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime})=\delta_{\zeta,\zeta^{\prime}}g_{\mathbf{k}}g_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}e^{-i(\theta_{\mathbf{k}}-\theta_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}})\zeta}\left[\alpha_{1}^{2}e^{-\frac{\absolutevalue{\vk-\vk'}^{2}\lambda_{1}^{2}}{4}}+\alpha_{2}^{2}\left(1-\frac{\absolutevalue{\vk-\vk'}^{2}\lambda_{1}^{2}}{4}\right)e^{-\frac{\absolutevalue{\vk-\vk'}^{2}\lambda_{2}^{2}}{4}}\right]+\delta_{\zeta,\zeta^{\prime}}h_{\mathbf{k}}h_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}.

(99)

In the limit
\lambda_{1}\approx\lambda_{2}
(which holds approximately for TBG near magic angle), the form factors can be simplified to

M_{\zeta\zeta^{\prime}}^{(\eta)}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime})\approx\delta_{\zeta,\zeta^{\prime}}g_{\mathbf{k}}g_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}e^{-i(\theta_{\mathbf{k}}-\theta_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}})\zeta}\left(1-\frac{\alpha_{2}^{2}\absolutevalue{\vk-\vk'}^{2}\lambda_{1}^{2}}{4}\right)e^{-\frac{\absolutevalue{\vk-\vk'}^{2}\lambda_{1}^{2}}{4}}+\delta_{\zeta,\zeta^{\prime}}h_{\mathbf{k}}h_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}\,.

(100)

B.2
The charge-one excitation spectrum of TBG in the
\absolutevalue{\gamma}>U_{1}
limit

Armed with analytical expressions of the active TBG bands in both the THF model basis and the TBG plane wave basis, we now show that the charge-one excitations of TBG can be obtained analytically in the
\absolutevalue{\gamma}>U_{1}
limit. We illustrate two different approaches to do so: one which leverages the results of Ref.
[
24
]
and employs the analytically derived TBG form factors from
Eq.

110
, and another one in which we project the THF interaction Hamiltonian within the active TBG bands using the analytical eigenstates of the THF single-particle Hamiltonian obtained in
Eq.

79
, within the zero-bandwidth limit.

B.2.1
Charge-one excitation matrices

As shown in Ref.
[
24
]
, for the integer-filled exact ground states of TBG derived by Ref.
[
23
]
, the charge-one excitation spectrum of TBG can be calculated directly, as we will review below. For a given exact eigenstate
\ket{\varphi}
of the projected interaction
H^{\text{TBG}}_{I}

[
23
]
, the charge-one excitation is obtained by evaluating the following commutators
[
24
]

\displaystyle\left[H^{\text{TBG}}_{I}-\mu\hat{N},\hat{d}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},\zeta,\eta,s}\right]\ket{\varphi}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\zeta^{\prime}}R^{\eta}_{\zeta^{\prime}\zeta}\left(\mathbf{k}\right)\hat{d}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},\zeta^{\prime},\eta,s}\ket{\varphi},

(101)

\displaystyle\left[H^{\text{TBG}}_{I}-\mu\hat{N},\hat{d}_{\mathbf{k},\zeta,\eta,s}\right]\ket{\varphi}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\zeta^{\prime}}\tilde{R}^{\eta}_{\zeta^{\prime}\zeta}\left(\mathbf{k}\right)\hat{d}_{\mathbf{k},\zeta^{\prime},\eta,s}\ket{\varphi},

(102)

where
\mu
is the chemical potential and
\hat{N}
is defined as

\hat{N}=\sum_{\mathbf{k},\zeta,\eta,s}\hat{d}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},\zeta,\eta,s}\hat{d}_{\mathbf{k},\zeta,\eta,s}.

(103)

The matrices
R^{\eta}_{\zeta^{\prime}\zeta}\left(\mathbf{k}\right)
and
\tilde{R}^{\eta}_{\zeta^{\prime}\zeta}\left(\mathbf{k}\right)
in
Eqs.

101
and
102
are determined by the filling
\nu
of the ground states
\ket{\varphi}
and by the form factors of
Eq.

88
. The explicit formulas are given below
[
24
]

\displaystyle R^{\eta}_{\zeta^{\prime}\zeta}\left(\mathbf{k}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\Omega_{0}N_{0}}\sum_{\mathbf{G}\in\mathcal{Q}_{0}}\left[\left(\sum_{\mathbf{q}\in\text{MBZ},\zeta^{\prime\prime}}V\left(\mathbf{q}+\mathbf{G}\right)M^{*(\eta)}_{\zeta^{\prime\prime}\zeta^{\prime}}\left(\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q}+\mathbf{G},\mathbf{k}\right)M^{(\eta)}_{\zeta^{\prime\prime}\zeta}\left(\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q}+\mathbf{G},\mathbf{k}\right)\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.+2A_{-\mathbf{G}}\sqrt{V\left(\mathbf{G}\right)}M^{(\eta)}_{\zeta^{\prime}\zeta}\left(\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{G},\mathbf{k}\right)\right]-\mu\delta_{\zeta^{\prime},\zeta},

(104)

\displaystyle\tilde{R}^{\eta}_{\zeta^{\prime}\zeta}\left(\mathbf{k}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\Omega_{0}N_{0}}\sum_{\mathbf{G}\in\mathcal{Q}_{0}}\left[\left(\sum_{\mathbf{q}\in\text{MBZ},\zeta^{\prime\prime}}V\left(\mathbf{q}+\mathbf{G}\right)M^{(\eta)}_{\zeta^{\prime\prime}\zeta^{\prime}}\left(\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q}+\mathbf{G},\mathbf{k}\right)M^{*(\eta)}_{\zeta^{\prime\prime}\zeta}\left(\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q}+\mathbf{G},\mathbf{k}\right)\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.-2A_{-\mathbf{G}}\sqrt{V\left(\mathbf{G}\right)}M^{*(\eta)}_{\zeta^{\prime}\zeta}\left(\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{G},\mathbf{k}\right)\right]+\mu\delta_{\zeta^{\prime},\zeta},

(105)

where
MBZ
denotes the moiré BZ and
A_{\mathbf{G}}
is defined as
[
24
]

A_{\mathbf{G}}=\sqrt{V\left(\mathbf{G}\right)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{k}\in\text{MBZ}\\
\zeta,\eta\end{subarray}}\frac{\nu}{4}M^{(\eta)}_{\zeta\zeta}\left(\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{G},\mathbf{k}\right)=\sqrt{V\left(\mathbf{G}\right)}\sum_{\mathbf{k}\in\text{MBZ}}\frac{\nu}{4}\text{Tr}\left[M\left(\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{G},\mathbf{k}\right)\right].

(106)

Using the fact that the approximate TBG form factors from
Eq.

110
are diagonal in the Chern band basis, we find that the corresponding charge-one excitations are also diagonal in the same basis,
i.e.

R^{\eta}_{\zeta^{\prime}\zeta}\left(\mathbf{k}\right)=\delta_{\zeta^{\prime},\zeta}R^{\eta}_{\zeta\zeta}\left(\mathbf{k}\right)
and
\tilde{R}^{\eta}_{\zeta^{\prime}\zeta}\left(\mathbf{k}\right)=\delta_{\zeta^{\prime},\zeta}\tilde{R}^{\eta}_{\zeta\zeta}\left(\mathbf{k}\right)
with

\displaystyle R^{\eta}_{\zeta\zeta}\left(\mathbf{k}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\Omega_{0}N_{0}}\sum_{\mathbf{G}\in\mathcal{Q}_{0}}\left[\left(\sum_{\mathbf{q}\in\text{MBZ}}V\left(\mathbf{q}+\mathbf{G}\right)M^{*(\eta)}_{\zeta\zeta}\left(\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q}+\mathbf{G},\mathbf{k}\right)M^{(\eta)}_{\zeta\zeta}\left(\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q}+\mathbf{G},\mathbf{k}\right)\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.+2A_{-\mathbf{G}}\sqrt{V\left(\mathbf{G}\right)}M^{(\eta)}_{\zeta\zeta}\left(\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{G},\mathbf{k}\right)\right]-\mu,

(107)

\displaystyle\tilde{R}^{\eta}_{\zeta\zeta}\left(\mathbf{k}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\Omega_{0}N_{0}}\sum_{\mathbf{G}\in\mathcal{Q}_{0}}\left[\left(\sum_{\mathbf{q}\in\text{MBZ}}V\left(\mathbf{q}+\mathbf{G}\right)M^{(\eta)}_{\zeta\zeta}\left(\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q}+\mathbf{G},\mathbf{k}\right)M^{*(\eta)}_{\zeta\zeta}\left(\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q}+\mathbf{G},\mathbf{k}\right)\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.-2A_{-\mathbf{G}}\sqrt{V\left(\mathbf{G}\right)}M^{*(\eta)}_{\zeta\zeta}\left(\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{G},\mathbf{k}\right)\right]+\mu.

(108)

At the same time, it is easy to see that
M^{*(\eta)}_{(-\zeta)(-\zeta)}\left(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}\right)=M^{(\eta)}_{\zeta\zeta}\left(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}\right)
, which implies that
A_{\mathbf{G}}
is real and that
R^{\eta}_{\zeta\zeta}\left(\mathbf{k}\right)=R^{*\eta}_{(-\zeta)(-\zeta)}\left(\mathbf{k}\right)
and
\tilde{R}^{\eta}_{\zeta\zeta}\left(\mathbf{k}\right)=\tilde{R}^{*\eta}_{(-\zeta)(-\zeta)}\left(\mathbf{k}\right)
. However, while the first term in each of
Eqs.

107
and
108
is real, the second term is not manifestly so. Generically, the TBG form factors obey the hermicitiy property
M^{(\eta)}_{\zeta\zeta^{\prime}}\left(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}\right)=M^{*(\eta)}_{\zeta^{\prime}\zeta}\left(\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\mathbf{k}\right)
, which follows trivially from their definition in
Eq.

88
. Together with the even symmetries of
A_{\mathbf{G}}=A_{-\mathbf{G}}
and
V(\mathbf{G})=V(-\mathbf{G})
, the hermiticity property of the form factors coupled with the periodicity in reciprocal space
M^{(\eta)}_{\zeta\zeta^{\prime}}\left(\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{G},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}+\mathbf{G}\right)=M^{(\eta)}_{\zeta\zeta^{\prime}}\left(\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{G},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}+\mathbf{G}\right)
is enough to guarantee the reality of the second term of each of
Eqs.

107
and
108
. However, the approximation in
Eq.

110
does not obey the periodicity property, which makes the charge-one excitation matrices nonhermitian at
\nu\neq 0
. For
\nu=0
(or alternatively when the so-called flat metric condition is imposed
[
24
]
at generic
\nu\neq 0
),
A_{\mathbf{G}}=0
, which implies that the charge-one excitation matrices are proportional to the identity matrix (and are therefore
bona-fide
hermitian matrices).

B.2.2
Analytical expressions for the charge-one excitation matrices of TBG

We now provide asymptotic analytical expression for the charge-one excitation matrices at
\nu=0
. The latter can be rewritten from
Eqs.

107
and
108
as

\displaystyle R^{\eta}_{\zeta\zeta}\left(\mathbf{k}\right)=\tilde{R}^{\eta}_{\zeta\zeta}\left(\mathbf{k}\right)=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\Omega_{0}N_{0}}\frac{\Omega_{0}N_{0}}{(2\pi)^{2}}\sum_{\mathbf{G}\in\mathcal{Q}_{0}}\int_{\text{MBZ}}V\left(\mathbf{q}+\mathbf{G}\right)\absolutevalue{M^{(\eta)}_{\zeta\zeta} \left(\vk+ \vq+ \vG, \vk\right)}^{2}\text{d}^{2}q

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{8\pi^{2}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}V\left(\mathbf{q}-\mathbf{k}\right)\absolutevalue{M^{(\eta)}_{\zeta\zeta} \left(\vq, \vk\right)}^{2}\text{d}q_{x}\text{d}q_{y}.

(109)

To move forward, we need to make two simplifying assumptions. First, we consider the
\sqrt{\absolutevalue{v_\star\vk}^{2}+\gamma^{2}e^{-|\mathbf{k}|^{2}\lambda^{2}}}
denominator of the
g_{\mathbf{k}}
and
h_{\mathbf{k}}
functions from
Eq.

80
, which appears in the expression of the form factors from
Eq.

110
. For small values of
\absolutevalue{\vk}
the exponential
e^{-\absolutevalue{\vk}^{2}\lambda^{2}}
is approximately 1. At large
\absolutevalue{\vk}
,
g_{\mathbf{k}}
is already multiplied by decaying exponentials in
Eq.

110
, while
h_{\mathbf{k}}
itself contains a decaying exponential factor in the numerator. In other words, for large
\absolutevalue{\vq}
, the exact values of the
g_{\mathbf{q}}
and
h_{\mathbf{q}}
functions is unimportant, as they become exponentially suppressed in the form factors. As a result, we will ignore the exponential decay factor appearing in the denominators of the
g_{\mathbf{q}}
and
h_{\mathbf{q}}
functions in
Eq.

109
and approximate the form factor as

M_{\zeta\zeta}^{(\eta)}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{q})=g_{\mathbf{k}}\tilde{g}_{\mathbf{q}}e^{-i(\theta_{\mathbf{k}}-\theta_{\mathbf{q}})\zeta}\left[\alpha_{1}^{2}e^{-\frac{\absolutevalue{\vk-\vq}^{2}\lambda_{1}^{2}}{4}}+\alpha_{2}^{2}\left(1-\frac{\absolutevalue{\vk-\vq}^{2}\lambda_{1}^{2}}{4}\right)e^{-\frac{\absolutevalue{\vk-\vq}^{2}\lambda_{2}^{2}}{4}}\right]+h_{\mathbf{k}}\tilde{h}_{\mathbf{q}},

(110)

where we have defined

\tilde{g}_{\mathbf{q}}\approx\frac{v_{\star}\absolutevalue{\vq}}{\sqrt{\absolutevalue{v_\star\vq}^{2}+\gamma^{2}}}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\tilde{h}_{\mathbf{q}}=\frac{\gamma e^{-|\mathbf{q}|^{2}\lambda^{2}/2}}{\sqrt{\absolutevalue{v_\star\vq}^{2}+\gamma^{2}}}.

(111)

Additionally, we note that, while the form factor decays exponentially in
\absolutevalue{\vq}
, the screened Coulomb potential only decays polynomially over the same momentum scale. As a simple approximation, we can can therefore approximate the Coulomb potential to a constant

V\left(\mathbf{q}-\mathbf{k}\right)\approx U_{\xi}\pi\xi^{2}.

(112)

Finally, we note that the integrand in
Eq.

109
only depends on the the relative angle between
\mathbf{k}
and
\mathbf{q}
and their magnitudes. Letting
\mathbf{k}=\left(k,0\right)
and
\mathbf{q}=\left(q\cos\theta,q\sin\theta\right)
without loss of generality, the charge-one excitation matrices become

\displaystyle R^{\eta}_{\zeta\zeta}\left(\mathbf{k}\right)=

\displaystyle\frac{U_{\xi}\xi^{2}}{8\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}q\text{d}q\int_{0}^{2\pi}\text{d}\theta\left(g^{2}_{k}\tilde{g}^{2}_{q}f^{2}\left(k,q,\theta\right)+h^{2}_{k}\tilde{h}^{2}_{q}+2g_{k}h_{k}\tilde{g}_{q}\tilde{h}_{q}f\left(k,q,\theta\right)\cos\theta\right),

(113)

with

f(k,q,\theta)=\alpha_{1}^{2}e^{-\frac{\lambda_{1}^{2}\left(k^{2}+q^{2}-2kq\cos\theta\right)}{4}}+\alpha_{2}^{2}\left(1-\frac{\lambda_{1}^{2}\left(k^{2}+q^{2}-2kq\cos\theta\right)}{4}\right)e^{-\frac{\lambda_{2}^{2}\left(k^{2}+q^{2}-2kq\cos\theta\right)}{4}}.

(114)

The integral over the angle in
Eq.

113
can be readily performed giving

\displaystyle R^{\eta}_{\zeta\zeta}\left(\mathbf{k}\right)=\frac{U_{\xi}\xi^{2}}{8\pi}\frac{1}{k^{2}v_{\star}^{2}+\gamma^{2}e^{-\frac{k^{2}\lambda^{2}}{2}}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{q\text{d}q}{q^{2}v_{\star}^{2}+\gamma^{2}}F(k,q),

(115)

where we have defined

\displaystyle F(k,q)=

\displaystyle 2\pi\gamma^{4}e^{-\lambda^{2}\left(k^{2}+q^{2}\right)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 2\pi\alpha_{1}^{4}k^{2}q^{2}v_{*}^{4}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{1}^{2}\left(k^{2}+q^{2}\right)}I_{0}\left(k\lambda_{1}^{2}q\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{8}\pi\alpha_{2}^{4}k^{2}q^{2}v_{*}^{4}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{2}^{2}\left(k^{2}+q^{2}\right)}\left(k^{2}\lambda_{1}^{2}+\lambda_{1}^{2}q^{2}-4\right){}^{2}I_{0}\left(k\lambda_{2}^{2}q\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\pi\alpha_{1}^{2}\alpha_{2}^{2}k^{2}q^{2}v_{*}^{4}\left(-e^{-\frac{1}{4}\left(\lambda_{1}^{2}+\lambda_{2}^{2}\right)\left(k^{2}+q^{2}\right)}\right)\left(k^{2}\lambda_{1}^{2}+\lambda_{1}^{2}q^{2}-4\right)I_{0}\left(\frac{1}{2}k\left(\lambda_{1}^{2}+\lambda_{2}^{2}\right)q\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 4\pi\alpha_{1}^{2}\gamma^{2}kqv_{*}^{2}e^{-\frac{1}{4}\left(2\lambda^{2}+\lambda_{1}^{2}\right)\left(k^{2}+q^{2}\right)}I_{1}\left(\frac{1}{2}k\lambda_{1}^{2}q\right)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{\pi\alpha_{2}^{2}\gamma^{2}kqv_{*}^{2}e^{-\frac{1}{4}\left(2\lambda^{2}+\lambda_{2}^{2}\right)\left(k^{2}+q^{2}\right)}\left(\lambda_{1}^{2}\left(k^{2}\lambda_{2}^{2}+\lambda_{2}^{2}q^{2}-4\right)-4\lambda_{2}^{2}\right)}{\lambda_{2}^{2}}I_{1}\left(\frac{1}{2}k\lambda_{2}^{2}q\right)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{\pi\alpha_{2}^{4}k^{3}\lambda_{1}^{2}q^{3}v_{*}^{4}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{2}^{2}\left(k^{2}+q^{2}\right)}\left(\lambda_{1}^{2}\left(k^{2}\lambda_{2}^{2}+\lambda_{2}^{2}q^{2}-1\right)-4\lambda_{2}^{2}\right)}{2\lambda_{2}^{2}}I_{1}\left(k\lambda_{2}^{2}q\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 2\pi\alpha_{1}^{2}\alpha_{2}^{2}k^{3}\lambda_{1}^{2}q^{3}v_{*}^{4}e^{-\frac{1}{4}\left(\lambda_{1}^{2}+\lambda_{2}^{2}\right)\left(k^{2}+q^{2}\right)}I_{1}\left(\frac{1}{2}k\left(\lambda_{1}^{2}+\lambda_{2}^{2}\right)q\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 2\pi\alpha_{2}^{2}\gamma^{2}k^{2}\lambda_{1}^{2}q^{2}v_{*}^{2}e^{-\frac{1}{4}\left(2\lambda^{2}+\lambda_{2}^{2}\right)\left(k^{2}+q^{2}\right)}I_{2}\left(\frac{1}{2}k\lambda_{2}^{2}q\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\pi\alpha_{2}^{4}k^{4}\lambda_{1}^{4}q^{4}v_{*}^{4}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{2}^{2}\left(k^{2}+q^{2}\right)}I_{2}\left(k\lambda_{2}^{2}q\right),

(116)

where
I_{n}\left(z\right)
is the
n
-th order modified Bessel function of the first kind. For the first term of
F(k,q)
, the integral over
q
can be directly performed

\int_{0}^{\infty}2\pi\gamma^{4}e^{-\lambda^{2}\left(k^{2}+q^{2}\right)}\frac{q\text{d}q}{q^{2}v_{\star}^{2}+\gamma^{2}}=\frac{\pi\gamma^{4}e^{-\lambda^{2}\left(k^{2}-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{v_{*}^{2}}\right)}\Gamma\left(0,\frac{\gamma^{2}\lambda^{2}}{v_{*}^{2}}\right)}{v_{*}^{2}}.

(117)

For the other terms, we apply the following strategy. First, we expand the Bessel functions according to

I_{n}(z)=\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{m!(m+n)!}\left(\frac{z}{2}\right)^{2m+n},\quad\mbox{for}\quad n\in\mathbb{N},

(118)

which is equivalent to a power series expansion in
q
of
F(k,q)
. We find it easier to perform this step for each term of
F(k,q)
separately. Consider the typical term in
Eq.

116
, which has the form
\mathcal{C}(k)q^{n_{1}}e^{-\frac{\Lambda_{1}^{2}}{2}q^{2}}I_{n_{2}}\left(\frac{kq\Lambda_{2}^{2}}{2}\right)
, where
\mathcal{C}(k)
is a function of
k
,
n_{1},n_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
, while
\Lambda_{1}
and
\Lambda_{2}
are combinations of
\lambda
,
\lambda_{1}
, or
\lambda_{2}
having dimension of length. For example, the second-to-last term of
Eq.

116
has this form with

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}(k)

\displaystyle=2\pi\alpha_{2}^{2}\gamma^{2}k^{2}\lambda_{1}^{2}v_{*}^{2}e^{-\frac{1}{4}\left(2\lambda^{2}+\lambda_{2}^{2}\right)k^{2}},

(119)

\displaystyle n_{1}=2,\quad n_{2}

\displaystyle=2,\quad\Lambda_{1}=\sqrt{2\lambda^{2}+\lambda^{2}_{2}},\quad\Lambda_{2}=\lambda^{2}_{2}.

(120)

Such a term can be integrated by first expanding the Bessel function according to
Eq.

118
and then integrating the resulting series in a term-by-term fashion

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{q\text{d}q}{q^{2}v_{\star}^{2}+\gamma^{2}}\mathcal{C}(k)q^{n_{1}}e^{-\frac{\Lambda_{1}^{2}}{2}q^{2}}I_{n_{2}}\left(\frac{kq\Lambda_{2}^{2}}{2}\right)=

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{v^{2}_{\star}}\mathcal{C}(k)\absolutevalue{\frac{\gamma}{v_{\star}}}^{n_{1}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{z\text{d}z}{1+z^{2}}z^{n_{1}}e^{-\frac{\Lambda_{1}^{2}\gamma^{2}}{2v^{2}_{\star}}z^{2}}I_{n_{2}}\left(kz\Lambda_{2}^{2}\absolutevalue{\frac{\gamma}{2 v_{\star}}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{v^{2}_{\star}}\mathcal{C}(k)\absolutevalue{\frac{\gamma}{v_{\star}}}^{n_{1}}\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{m!(m+n_{2})!}\left(k\Lambda_{2}^{2}\absolutevalue{\frac{\gamma}{4 v_{\star}}}\right)^{2m+n_{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{z^{n_{1}+n_{2}+2m+1}\text{d}z}{1+z^{2}}e^{-\frac{\Lambda_{1}^{2}\gamma^{2}}{2v^{2}_{\star}}z^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{v^{2}_{\star}}\mathcal{C}(k)\absolutevalue{\frac{\gamma}{v_{\star}}}^{n_{1}}\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{n_{1}+n_{2}}{2}+m+1\right)}{m!(m+n_{2})!}\left(k\Lambda_{2}^{2}\absolutevalue{\frac{\gamma}{4 v_{\star}}}\right)^{2m+n_{2}}\frac{1}{2}e^{\frac{\Lambda_{1}^{2}\gamma^{2}}{2v^{2}_{\star}}}\Gamma\left(-\frac{n_{1}+n_{2}}{2}-m,\frac{\Lambda_{1}^{2}\gamma^{2}}{4v^{2}_{\star}}\right).

(121)

We now note that for all the terms in
Eq.

116
,
\frac{\Lambda_{1}^{2}\gamma^{2}}{2v^{2}_{\star}}\ll 1
, suggesting that the incomplete Gamma function can be expanded in the second argument. Moreover, we find that for all the terms in
Eq.

116
proportional to a Bessel function,
n_{1}+n_{2}\geq 2
is even. As such, we can employ the following representation of the incomplete Gamma function

\Gamma(-m,z)=\frac{(-1)^{m}}{m!}\left[\Gamma(0,z)-e^{-z}\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}\frac{j!(-1)^{j}}{z^{j+1}}\right],\quad\mbox{for}\quad m\in\mathbb{N}.

(122)

For small
z
, the incomplete Gamma function has the following asymptotic form

\Gamma(-m,z)=\frac{1}{mz^{m}}+\begin{cases}\mathcal{O}\left(z^{-m+1}\right),&\quad m\geq 1\\
\mathcal{O}\left(\log z\right),&\quad m=1\\
\end{cases}

(123)

which enables us to rewrite the integral in
Eq.

121
as

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{q\text{d}q}{q^{2}v_{\star}^{2}+\gamma^{2}}\mathcal{C}(k)q^{n_{1}}e^{-\frac{\Lambda_{1}^{2}}{2}q^{2}}I_{n_{2}}\left(\frac{kq\Lambda_{2}^{2}}{2}\right)=

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{v^{2}_{\star}}\mathcal{C}(k)\absolutevalue{\frac{\gamma}{v_{\star}}}^{n_{1}}\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{n_{1}+n_{2}}{2}+m+1\right)}{m!(m+n_{2})!}\left(k\Lambda_{2}^{2}\absolutevalue{\frac{\gamma}{4 v_{\star}}}\right)^{2m+n_{2}}

\displaystyle\times\left[\frac{\left(\frac{\Lambda_{1}^{2}\gamma^{2}}{2v^{2}_{\star}}\right)^{-\frac{n_{1}+n_{2}}{2}-m}}{n_{1}+n_{2}+2m}+\mathcal{O}\left(\left(\frac{\Lambda_{1}^{2}\gamma^{2}}{2v^{2}_{\star}}\right)^{-\frac{n_{1}+n_{2}}{2}-m+1}+\log\left(\frac{\Lambda_{1}^{2}\gamma^{2}}{2v^{2}_{\star}}\right)\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{v^{2}_{\star}}\mathcal{C}(k)\absolutevalue{\frac{\gamma}{v_{\star}}}^{n_{1}}\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{n_{1}+n_{2}}{2}+m+1\right)}{m!(m+n_{2})!}\left(k\Lambda_{2}^{2}\absolutevalue{\frac{\gamma}{4 v_{\star}}}\right)^{n_{2}}\left(\frac{k^{2}\Lambda_{2}^{2}}{2\sqrt{2}}\right)^{m}\left(\frac{\Lambda_{2}}{\Lambda_{1}}\right)^{m}\left(\frac{\Lambda_{1}^{2}\gamma^{2}}{2v^{2}_{\star}}\right)^{m}

\displaystyle\times\left[\frac{\left(\frac{\Lambda_{1}^{2}\gamma^{2}}{2v^{2}_{\star}}\right)^{-\frac{n_{1}+n_{2}}{2}-m}}{n_{1}+n_{2}+2m}+\mathcal{O}\left(\left(\frac{\Lambda_{1}^{2}\gamma^{2}}{2v^{2}_{\star}}\right)^{-\frac{n_{1}+n_{2}}{2}-m+1}+\log\left(\frac{\Lambda_{1}^{2}\gamma^{2}}{2v^{2}_{\star}}\right)\right)\right].

(124)

Keeping only the leading contribution in the small parameter
\frac{\Lambda_{1}^{2}\gamma^{2}}{2v^{2}_{\star}}
, we can approximate the integral from
Eq.

121
as

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{q\text{d}q}{q^{2}v_{\star}^{2}+\gamma^{2}}\mathcal{C}(k)q^{n_{1}}e^{-\frac{\Lambda_{1}^{2}}{2}q^{2}}I_{n_{2}}\left(\frac{kq\Lambda_{2}^{2}}{2}\right)\approx

\displaystyle\approx\frac{1}{v^{2}_{\star}}\mathcal{C}(k)\absolutevalue{\frac{\gamma}{v_{\star}}}^{n_{1}}\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{n_{1}+n_{2}}{2}+m+1\right)}{m!(m+n_{2})!}\left(k\Lambda_{2}^{2}\absolutevalue{\frac{\gamma}{4 v_{\star}}}\right)^{n_{2}}\left(\frac{k^{2}\Lambda_{2}^{2}}{2\sqrt{2}}\right)^{m}\left(\frac{\Lambda_{2}}{\Lambda_{1}}\right)^{m}\frac{\left(\frac{\Lambda_{1}^{2}\gamma^{2}}{2v^{2}_{\star}}\right)^{-\frac{n_{1}+n_{2}}{2}}}{n_{1}+n_{2}+2m}.

(125)

Finally, the series in
Eq.

125
can be re-summed into an elementary function. Performing this approximation for each term in
Eq.

116
, we arrive at the following result

\displaystyle\frac{8\pi}{U_{\xi}\xi^{2}}\left(k^{2}v_{\star}^{2}+\gamma^{2}e^{-\frac{k^{2}\lambda^{2}}{2}}\right)R^{\eta}_{\zeta\zeta}\left(\mathbf{k}\right)\approx

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\frac{\pi\gamma^{4}e^{-\lambda^{2}\left(k^{2}-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{v_{*}^{2}}\right)}\Gamma\left(0,\frac{\gamma^{2}\lambda^{2}}{v_{*}^{2}}\right)}{v_{*}^{2}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{\pi\alpha_{2}^{4}k^{2}\lambda_{1}^{4}\left(k^{4}\lambda_{2}^{4}-8k^{2}\lambda_{2}^{2}+8\right)v_{*}^{2}}{8\lambda_{2}^{6}}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{\pi\alpha_{2}^{4}k^{2}\lambda_{1}^{2}\left(\lambda_{1}^{2}\lambda_{2}^{2}k^{4}-4\left(\lambda_{1}^{2}+\lambda_{2}^{2}\right)k^{2}+8\right)v_{*}^{2}}{4\lambda_{2}^{4}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{2\pi\alpha_{2}^{2}\gamma^{2}\lambda_{1}^{2}e^{-\frac{1}{4}k^{2}\left(2\lambda^{2}+\lambda_{2}^{2}\right)}\left(e^{\frac{k^{2}\lambda_{2}^{4}}{8\lambda^{2}+4\lambda_{2}^{2}}}\left(4\lambda_{2}^{2}\lambda^{2}\left(k^{2}\lambda_{2}^{2}-8\right)+\lambda_{2}^{4}\left(k^{2}\lambda_{2}^{2}-8\right)-32\lambda^{4}\right)+8\left(2\lambda^{2}+\lambda_{2}^{2}\right){}^{2}\right)}{\lambda_{2}^{4}\left(2\lambda^{2}+\lambda_{2}^{2}\right){}^{2}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{2\pi\alpha_{1}^{2}\alpha_{2}^{2}k^{2}\lambda_{1}^{2}\left(k^{2}\left(\lambda_{1}^{2}+\lambda_{2}^{2}\right)-4\right)v_{*}^{2}}{\left(\lambda_{1}^{2}+\lambda_{2}^{2}\right){}^{2}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{2\pi\alpha_{1}^{4}k^{2}v_{*}^{2}}{\lambda_{1}^{2}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{8\pi\alpha_{1}^{2}\gamma^{2}e^{-\frac{1}{4}k^{2}\left(2\lambda^{2}+\lambda_{1}^{2}\right)}\left(e^{\frac{k^{2}\lambda_{1}^{4}}{8\lambda^{2}+4\lambda_{1}^{2}}}-1\right)}{\lambda_{1}^{2}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{\pi\alpha_{2}^{4}k^{2}\left(k^{2}\lambda_{1}^{2}-4\right){}^{2}v_{*}^{2}}{8\lambda_{2}^{2}}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{2\pi\alpha_{2}^{2}\gamma^{2}e^{-\frac{1}{4}k^{2}\left(2\lambda^{2}+\lambda_{2}^{2}\right)}\left(e^{\frac{k^{2}\lambda_{2}^{4}}{8\lambda^{2}+4\lambda_{2}^{2}}}-1\right)\left(\lambda_{1}^{2}\left(k^{2}\lambda_{2}^{2}-4\right)-4\lambda_{2}^{2}\right)}{\lambda_{2}^{4}}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{2\pi\alpha_{1}^{2}\alpha_{2}^{2}k^{2}\left(k^{2}\lambda_{1}^{2}-4\right)v_{*}^{2}}{\lambda_{1}^{2}+\lambda_{2}^{2}}.

(126)

To simplify the expression in
Eq.

126
, we now take the approximation
\lambda_{1}\approx\lambda_{2}\approx\frac{\lambda}{\sqrt{2}}
, which holds approximately for TBG around the magic angle and realistic interlayer tunneling amplitudes
w_{0}/w_{1}=0.8
. Coupled with the normalization condition
\alpha_{1}^{2}+\alpha_{2}^{2}=1
, this enables us to obtain the following approximate expression for the charge-one excitation matrix

\displaystyle\frac{k^{2}v_{\star}^{2}+\gamma^{2}e^{-\frac{k^{2}\lambda^{2}}{2}}}{U_{\xi}\xi^{2}}R^{\eta}_{\zeta\zeta}\left(\mathbf{k}\right)\approx

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\frac{\pi\gamma^{4}e^{-\lambda^{2}\left(k^{2}-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{v_{*}^{2}}\right)}\Gamma\left(0,\frac{\gamma^{2}\lambda^{2}}{v_{*}^{2}}\right)}{v_{*}^{2}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{-25\gamma^{2}e^{-\frac{5}{8}k^{2}\lambda^{2}}\left(\alpha_{1}^{2}\left(k^{2}\lambda^{2}+8\right)-k^{2}\lambda^{2}\right)+8\gamma^{2}e^{-\frac{3}{5}k^{2}\lambda^{2}}\left(\alpha_{1}^{2}\left(2k^{2}\lambda^{2}+25\right)-2k^{2}\lambda^{2}\right)+25\left(\alpha_{1}^{4}+1\right)k^{2}v_{*}^{2}}{100\lambda^{2}}.

(127)

The simplified expression in
Eq.

127
readily explains the shape of the charge-one excitation spectrum around charge neutrality

\frac{8R^{\eta}_{\zeta\zeta}\left(\mathbf{k}\right)}{U_{\xi}\xi^{2}}=\begin{cases}\frac{2\left(\alpha_{1}^{4}+1\right)}{\lambda^{2}},\quad\mbox{for}\quad k\to\infty\\
k^{2}\left(\frac{2}{25}\left(-4\alpha_{1}^{2}+\frac{25\left(\alpha_{1}^{4}+1\right)v_{*}^{2}}{\gamma^{2}\lambda^{2}}+9\right)-e^{\frac{\gamma^{2}\lambda^{2}}{v_{*}^{2}}}\Gamma\left(0,\frac{\gamma^{2}\lambda^{2}}{v_{*}^{2}}\right)\right)+\frac{\gamma^{2}e^{\frac{\gamma^{2}\lambda^{2}}{v_{*}^{2}}}\Gamma\left(0,\frac{\gamma^{2}\lambda^{2}}{v_{*}^{2}}\right)}{v_{*}^{2}},\quad\mbox{for}\quad k\approx 0\end{cases}.

(128)

We benchmark the analytical expressions for the TBG form factors from
Eq.

110
by computing the charge-one excitation dispersion of TBG. We focus on charge neutrality, where the dispersion is identical for both the electron- and hole-excitations and compute the corresponding charge-one excitation matrices both exactly using
Eqs.

107
and
108
and analytically using the asmyptotic expression derived in
Eq.

127
.
The resulting charge-one excitation dispersion is shown in
Fig.

8
and qualitatively agrees with the numerical results of Ref.
[
24
]
.

Figure 8:
Charge-one excitations of TBG above the
\nu=0
correlated insulators computed with the analytical form-factor expression from
Eq.

110
. We take
\theta=$1.05\text{\,}\mathrm{\SIUnitSymbolDegree}$
and
w_{0}/w_{1}=0.5
for which the relevant THF model parameters are given by
\alpha_{1}=0.9208
,
\alpha_{2}=0.3901
,
\lambda=0.533\absolutevalue{\vec{a}_{M1}}
,
\lambda_{1}=0.2268\absolutevalue{\vec{a}_{M1}}
,
\lambda_{2}=0.2089\absolutevalue{\vec{a}_{M1}}
,
\absolutevalue{\gamma}=$65.78\text{\,}\mathrm{meV}$
, and
U_{1}=$37.61\text{\,}\mathrm{meV}$
(such that the condition
\absolutevalue{\gamma}>U_{1}
is satisfied). The blue line show the dispersion computed using the formula from
Eqs.

107
and
108
, while the orange line corresponds to its analytical asymptotic expression from
Eq.

127
.

B.2.3
Flat-band projected limit of the THF model

The THF model has been used in Refs.
[
33
,
273
,
274
,
45
,
276
,
277
,
50
,
275
,
49
,
278
,
171
]
to obtain many of the properties of TBG in the limit
U_{1}>\absolutevalue{\gamma}
. In this section, we discuss the opposite limit – the flat-band projected limit of the THF model – which is valid for
U_{1}<\absolutevalue{\gamma}
. Within this parameter regime, the remote bands are effectively gapped degrees of freedom and the interaction THF Hamiltonian can be projected into the active TBG bands. We will show that in the limit
U_{1}<\absolutevalue{\gamma}
, the THF model remains a reliable mapping of the TBG problem, which captures the essential physics of the system, even though the interaction cannot be treated locally anymore, due to the heavy mixing between the
c
- and
f
-electrons.

In what follows, we analytically obtain the charge-
(\pm 1)
excitation of the system around integer fillings, in the limit of
v_{\star}^{\prime}=v_{\star}^{\prime\prime}=0
,
M=0
and
U_{1}<\absolutevalue{\gamma}
. As already discussed near
Eq.

79
, the single-particle Hamiltonian of the system can be solved analytically. For
U_{1}<\absolutevalue{\gamma}
, the remote bands corresponding to
\hat{b}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},j,\eta,s}
are high-energy degrees of freedom, being separated from the active TBG bands by
\absolutevalue{\gamma}
. At integer fillings
-4\leq\nu\leq+4
, the ground states of the system were shown to be Slater determinants obtained by fully filling the TBG Chern bands
[
23
]

|\Psi_{\nu}\rangle=\prod_{\mathbf{k}}\prod_{n=1}^{\nu+4}\hat{d}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},\zeta_{n},\eta_{n},s_{n}}|\text{RB}\rangle.

(129)

where
\{\xi_{n},\eta_{n},s_{n}\}_{n}
are
(\nu+4)
flavor indices arbitrarily sorted in
n
and
|\text{RB}\rangle
is the Slater determinant state corresponding to the fully occupied remote bands with negative energy

|\text{RB}\rangle=\prod_{\mathbf{k}}\prod_{\eta,s,\absolutevalue{j}=1}\hat{b}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},j,\eta,s}|0\rangle\,.

(130)

For the given ground state, we can then obtain the charge
\pm 1
excitation via the Hartree-Fock approximation
[
50
,
33
]
. In the Hartree-Fock approach, we can rewrite the interacting Hamiltonian as

H_{I,\text{MF}}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,\eta,s\\
i^{\prime},\eta^{\prime},s^{\prime}\end{subarray}}h^{I,\text{MF}}_{i\eta s;i^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}\left(\mathbf{k}\right)\hat{\gamma}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},i,\eta,s}\hat{\gamma}_{\mathbf{k},i^{\prime},\eta^{\prime},s^{\prime}},

(131)

where we have introduced the following operators to simplify the notations

\hat{\gamma}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},\eta,i,s}\equiv\begin{cases}\hat{c}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},\eta,i,s},&\quad\mbox{for}\quad 1\leq i\leq 4\\
\hat{f}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},\eta,i-4,s},&\quad\mbox{for}\quad 5\leq i\leq 6\end{cases}.

(132)

We consider the full THF interacting Hamiltonain from
Eq.

66
, but follow Ref.
[
203
]
and only consider the Hartree contribution stemming from
H_{V}
, but otherwise include both the Hartree and Fock contributions stemming from the other terms. The Hartree-Fock Hamiltonian is then characterized by the following matrix
[
50
]

\displaystyle h^{I,\text{MF}}_{i\eta s;i^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}\left(\mathbf{k}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\alpha=1}^{2}\left(U_{1}\nu_{f}+3U_{2}\nu_{f}+\frac{1}{N_{0}}\sum_{a=1}^{4}W_{a}\sum_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}\sum_{\eta^{\prime\prime},s^{\prime\prime}}\varrho_{a\eta^{\prime\prime}s^{\prime\prime};a\eta^{\prime\prime}s^{\prime\prime}}\left(\mathbf{k}^{\prime}\right)\right)\delta_{(\alpha+4)i}\delta_{(\alpha+4)i^{\prime}}\delta_{\eta\eta^{\prime}}\delta_{ss^{\prime}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{a=1}^{4}\left(V(\mathbf{0})\nu_{c}+W_{a}\nu_{f}\right)\delta_{ai}\delta_{ai^{\prime}}\delta_{\eta\eta^{\prime}}\delta_{ss^{\prime}}

\displaystyle-\frac{U}{N_{0}}\sum_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}=1}^{2}\delta_{(\alpha+4)i}\delta_{(\alpha^{\prime}+4)i^{\prime}}\sum_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}\varrho_{i^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime};i\eta s}\left(\mathbf{k}^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{U_{2}}{N_{0}}\sum_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}\sum_{n=0}^{5}\cos\left((\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime})\cdot C_{6z}^{n}\mathbf{a}_{M_{1}}\right)\varrho_{i^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime};i\eta s}(\mathbf{k}^{\prime})

\displaystyle-\sum_{\alpha=1}^{2}\sum_{a^{\prime}=1}^{4}\frac{W_{a^{\prime}}}{N_{0}}\left[\delta_{(\alpha+4)i}\delta_{a^{\prime}i^{\prime}}+\delta_{(\alpha+4)i^{\prime}}\delta_{a^{\prime}i}\right]\sum_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}\varrho_{i^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime};i\eta s}\left(\mathbf{k}^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{N_{0}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1},\eta_{1},\sigma_{1}\\
i_{2},\eta_{2},\sigma_{2}\end{subarray}}\left(\mathcal{J}_{i_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1};i_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2};i\eta s;i^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}+\mathcal{J}_{i\eta s;i^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime};i_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1};i_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}\right)\sum_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}\varrho_{i_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1};i_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}\left(\mathbf{k}^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{N_{0}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1},\eta_{1},\sigma_{1}\\
i_{2},\eta_{2},\sigma_{2}\end{subarray}}\left(\mathcal{J}_{i_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1};i^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime};i\eta s;i_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}+\mathcal{J}_{i\eta s;i_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2};i_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}i^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}\right)\sum_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}\varrho_{i_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1};i_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}\left(\mathbf{k}^{\prime}\right),

(133)

where the interaction tensor is defined as

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{i_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1};i_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2};i_{3}\eta_{3}s_{3};i_{4}\eta_{4}s_{4}}=-\frac{J}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}=1\\
\eta,\eta^{\prime}\end{subarray}}^{2}\left[\eta\eta^{\prime}+\left(-1\right)^{\alpha+\alpha^{\prime}}\right]\delta_{(\alpha+4)i_{1}}\delta_{(\alpha^{\prime}+4)i_{2}}\delta_{(\alpha^{\prime}+2)i_{3}}\delta_{(\alpha+2)i_{4}}\delta_{\eta\eta_{1}}\delta_{\eta^{\prime}\eta_{2}}\delta_{\eta^{\prime}\eta_{3}}\delta_{\eta\eta_{4}}\delta_{s_{1}s_{4}}\delta_{s_{2}s_{3}}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{J}{4}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}=1\\
\eta,\eta^{\prime}\end{subarray}}^{2}\left[\eta\eta^{\prime}-\left(-1\right)^{\alpha+\alpha^{\prime}}\right]\delta_{(\alpha+4)i_{1}}\delta_{(\alpha^{\prime}+2)i_{2}}\delta_{(\alpha^{\prime}+4)i_{3}}\delta_{(\alpha+2)i_{4}}\delta_{\eta\eta_{1}}\delta_{\eta^{\prime}\eta_{2}}\delta_{\eta^{\prime}\eta_{3}}\delta_{\eta\eta_{4}}\delta_{s_{1}s_{4}}\delta_{s_{2}s_{3}}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{J}{4}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}=1\\
\eta,\eta^{\prime}\end{subarray}}^{2}\left[\eta\eta^{\prime}-\left(-1\right)^{\alpha+\alpha^{\prime}}\right]\delta_{(\alpha+4)i_{2}}\delta_{(\alpha^{\prime}+2)i_{1}}\delta_{(\alpha^{\prime}+4)i_{4}}\delta_{(\alpha+2)i_{3}}\delta_{\eta\eta_{1}}\delta_{\eta^{\prime}\eta_{2}}\delta_{\eta^{\prime}\eta_{3}}\delta_{\eta\eta_{4}}\delta_{s_{1}s_{4}}\delta_{s_{2}s_{3}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{K}{\Omega_{0}}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{2}\sum_{\eta\eta^{\prime}}\eta\eta^{\prime}[\delta_{\bar{\alpha}i_{1}}\delta_{(\alpha+2)i_{2}}\delta_{(\alpha+4)i_{3}}\delta_{(\alpha+2)i_{4}}\delta_{\eta\eta_{1}}\delta_{\eta\eta_{2}}\delta_{\eta^{\prime}\eta_{3}}\delta_{\eta^{\prime}\eta_{4}}\delta_{s_{1}s_{2}}\delta_{s_{3}s_{4}}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\delta_{(\alpha+4)i_{1}}\delta_{(\alpha+2)i_{2}}\delta_{(\bar{\alpha}+2)i_{3}}\delta_{\alpha i_{4}}\delta_{\eta\eta_{1}}\delta_{\eta\eta_{2}}\delta_{\eta^{\prime}\eta_{3}}\delta_{\eta^{\prime}\eta_{4}}\delta_{s_{1}s_{2}}\delta_{s_{3}s_{4}}]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{K}{\Omega_{0}}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{2}\sum_{\eta\eta^{\prime}}\eta\eta^{\prime}[\delta_{\bar{\alpha}i_{4}}\delta_{(\alpha+2)i_{3}}\delta_{(\alpha+4)i_{2}}\delta_{(\alpha+2)i_{1}}\delta_{\eta\eta_{4}}\delta_{\eta\eta_{3}}\delta_{\eta^{\prime}\eta_{2}}\delta_{\eta^{\prime}\eta_{1}}\delta_{s_{1}s_{2}}\delta_{s_{3}s_{4}}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\delta_{(\alpha+4)i_{4}}\delta_{(\alpha+2)i_{3}}\delta_{(\bar{\alpha}+2)i_{2}}\delta_{\alpha i_{1}}\delta_{\eta\eta_{4}}\delta_{\eta\eta_{3}}\delta_{\eta^{\prime}\eta_{2}}\delta_{\eta^{\prime}\eta_{1}}\delta_{s_{1}s_{2}}\delta_{s_{3}s_{4}}]

(134)

The Hartree-Fock Hamiltonian also depends on the density matrix of the system

\varrho_{i\eta s;i^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}\left(\mathbf{k}\right)=\left\langle:\mathrel{\hat{\gamma}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},i,\eta,s}\hat{\gamma}_{\mathbf{k},i^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}}:\right\rangle\,,

(135)

and the filling of the
f
-electrons (
\nu_{f}
) and
c
-electrons (
\nu_{c}
)

\nu_{c}=\frac{1}{N_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{4}\sum_{\mathbf{k},\eta,\sigma}\varrho_{i\eta\sigma;i\eta\sigma}\left(\mathbf{k}\right)\quad\mbox{and}\quad\nu_{f}=\frac{1}{N_{0}}\sum_{i=5}^{6}\sum_{\mathbf{k},\eta,\sigma}\varrho_{i\eta\sigma;i\eta\sigma}\left(\mathbf{k}\right)\,.

(136)

The total filling of the system is
\nu=\nu_{f}+\nu_{c}
.

For the Slater-determinant ground states from
Eq.

129
, we candirectly compute the density matrix using
Eq.

135
and then diagonalize the Hartree-Fock Hamiltonian to get the charge-(
\pm 1
) excitation spectra.
Here, we also provide the density matrix for different integer fillings. We find that the density matrix is diagonal in the valley and spin spaces with

\varrho_{i\eta s;i^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}\left(\mathbf{k}\right)=\delta_{\eta,\eta^{\prime}}\delta_{s,s^{\prime}}\varrho_{\eta,s,ij}

(137)

where
\varrho_{\eta,s,ij}
is the density matrix of valley
\eta
spin
s
block. For each block, we have four cases corresponding to four different density matrices. The four cases are: (1) filling zero
\hat{d}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},\zeta,\eta,s}
electrons (
\varrho^{\text{empty}}_{\eta,s,ij}(\mathbf{k})
); (2) filling one
\hat{d}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},+,\eta,s}
electron for each
\mathbf{k}
(
\varrho^{+}_{\eta,s,ij}(\mathbf{k})
); (3) filling one
\hat{d}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},-,\eta,s}
electron for each
\mathbf{k}
(
\varrho^{-}_{\eta,s,ij}(\mathbf{k})
); (4) filling one
\hat{d}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},+,\eta,s}
electron and one
\hat{d}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},-,\eta,s}
electron for each
\mathbf{k}
(
\varrho^{+-}_{\eta,s,ij}(\mathbf{k})
). The corresponding density matrices are

\displaystyle\varrho_{\eta,s}^{\text{empty}}(\mathbf{k})

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}0&0&-\frac{1}{2}\eta g_{\mathbf{k}}e^{-i\eta\theta_{\mathbf{k}}}&0&-\frac{h_{\mathbf{k}}}{2}&0\\
0&0&0&-\frac{1}{2}\eta g_{\mathbf{k}}e^{i\eta\theta_{\mathbf{k}}}&0&-\frac{h_{\mathbf{k}}}{2}\\
-\frac{1}{2}\eta g_{\mathbf{k}}e^{i\eta\theta_{\mathbf{k}}}&0&\frac{g_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}-1}{2}&0&\frac{1}{2}\eta g_{\mathbf{k}}h_{\mathbf{k}}e^{i\eta\theta_{\mathbf{k}}}&0\\
0&-\frac{1}{2}\eta g_{\mathbf{k}}e^{-i\eta\theta_{\mathbf{k}}}&0&\frac{g_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}-1}{2}&0&\frac{1}{2}\eta g_{\mathbf{k}}h_{\mathbf{k}}e^{-i\eta\theta_{\mathbf{k}}}\\
-\frac{h_{\mathbf{k}}}{2}&0&\frac{1}{2}\eta g_{\mathbf{k}}h_{\mathbf{k}}e^{-i\eta\theta_{\mathbf{k}}}&0&\frac{h_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}-1}{2}&0\\
0&-\frac{h_{\mathbf{k}}}{2}&0&\frac{1}{2}\eta g_{\mathbf{k}}h_{\mathbf{k}}e^{i\eta\theta_{\mathbf{k}}}&0&\frac{h_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}-1}{2}\\
\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle\varrho_{\eta,s}^{+}(\mathbf{k})

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}0&0&-\frac{1}{2}\eta g_{\mathbf{k}}e^{-i\eta\theta_{\mathbf{k}}}&0&-\frac{h_{\mathbf{k}}}{2}&0\\
0&0&0&-\frac{1}{2}\eta g_{\mathbf{k}}e^{i\eta\theta_{\mathbf{k}}}&0&-\frac{h_{\mathbf{k}}}{2}\\
-\frac{1}{2}\eta g_{\mathbf{k}}e^{i\eta\theta_{\mathbf{k}}}&0&\frac{1}{2}h_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}&0&-\frac{1}{2}\eta g_{\mathbf{k}}h_{\mathbf{k}}e^{i\eta\theta_{\mathbf{k}}}&0\\
0&-\frac{1}{2}\eta g_{\mathbf{k}}e^{-i\eta\theta_{\mathbf{k}}}&0&\frac{g_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}-1}{2}&0&\frac{1}{2}\eta g_{\mathbf{k}}h_{\mathbf{k}}e^{-i\eta\theta_{\mathbf{k}}}\\
-\frac{h_{\mathbf{k}}}{2}&0&-\frac{1}{2}\eta g_{\mathbf{k}}h_{\mathbf{k}}e^{-i\eta\theta_{\mathbf{k}}}&0&\frac{g_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}}{2}&0\\
0&-\frac{h_{\mathbf{k}}}{2}&0&\frac{1}{2}\eta g_{\mathbf{k}}h_{\mathbf{k}}e^{i\eta\theta_{\mathbf{k}}}&0&\frac{h_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}-1}{2}\\
\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle\varrho_{\eta,s}^{-}(\mathbf{k})

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}0&0&-\frac{1}{2}\eta g_{\mathbf{k}}e^{-i\eta\theta_{\mathbf{k}}}&0&-\frac{h_{\mathbf{k}}}{2}&0\\
0&0&0&-\frac{1}{2}\eta g_{\mathbf{k}}e^{i\eta\theta_{\mathbf{k}}}&0&-\frac{h_{\mathbf{k}}}{2}\\
-\frac{1}{2}\eta g_{\mathbf{k}}e^{i\eta\theta_{\mathbf{k}}}&0&\frac{g_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}-1}{2}&0&\frac{1}{2}\eta g_{\mathbf{k}}h_{\mathbf{k}}e^{i\eta\theta_{\mathbf{k}}}&0\\
0&-\frac{1}{2}\eta g_{\mathbf{k}}e^{-i\eta\theta_{\mathbf{k}}}&0&\frac{1}{2}h_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}&0&-\frac{1}{2}\eta g_{\mathbf{k}}h_{\mathbf{k}}e^{-i\eta\theta_{\mathbf{k}}}\\
-\frac{h_{\mathbf{k}}}{2}&0&\frac{1}{2}\eta g_{\mathbf{k}}h_{\mathbf{k}}e^{-i\eta\theta_{\mathbf{k}}}&0&\frac{h_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}-1}{2}&0\\
0&-\frac{h_{\mathbf{k}}}{2}&0&-\frac{1}{2}\eta g_{\mathbf{k}}h_{\mathbf{k}}e^{i\eta\theta_{\mathbf{k}}}&0&\frac{g_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}}{2}\\
\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle\varrho_{\eta,s}^{+-}(\mathbf{k})

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}0&0&-\frac{1}{2}\eta g_{\mathbf{k}}e^{-i\eta\theta_{\mathbf{k}}}&0&-\frac{h_{\mathbf{k}}}{2}&0\\
0&0&0&-\frac{1}{2}\eta g_{\mathbf{k}}e^{i\eta\theta_{\mathbf{k}}}&0&-\frac{h_{\mathbf{k}}}{2}\\
-\frac{1}{2}\eta g_{\mathbf{k}}e^{i\eta\theta_{\mathbf{k}}}&0&\frac{1}{2}h_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}&0&-\frac{1}{2}\eta g_{\mathbf{k}}h_{\mathbf{k}}e^{i\eta\theta_{\mathbf{k}}}&0\\
0&-\frac{1}{2}\eta g_{\mathbf{k}}e^{-i\eta\theta_{\mathbf{k}}}&0&\frac{1}{2}h_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}&0&-\frac{1}{2}\eta g_{\mathbf{k}}h_{\mathbf{k}}e^{-i\eta\theta_{\mathbf{k}}}\\
-\frac{h_{\mathbf{k}}}{2}&0&-\frac{1}{2}\eta g_{\mathbf{k}}h_{\mathbf{k}}e^{-i\eta\theta_{\mathbf{k}}}&0&\frac{1}{2}g_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}&0\\
0&-\frac{h_{\mathbf{k}}}{2}&0&-\frac{1}{2}\eta g_{\mathbf{k}}h_{\mathbf{k}}e^{i\eta\theta_{\mathbf{k}}}&0&\frac{1}{2}g_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}\\
\end{array}\right)

Using the above density matrices, one can directly obtain the charge-(
\pm 1
) excitations by diagaonalizing the corresponding Hartree-Fock Hamiltonian obtained using
Eq.

133
.

In
Fig.

9
, we show the charge-(
\pm 1
) excitation at fillings integer filling
-3\leq\nu\leq 0
of THF model for
w_{0}/w_{1}=0.5
. For this tunneling ratio, the Hubbard interaction
U_{1}=$37.61\text{\,}\mathrm{meV}$
is smaller than the
f
-
c
hybridization strength
\absolutevalue{\gamma}=$65.78\text{\,}\mathrm{meV}$
. For the other parameters, we employed
[
33
]

\displaystyle v_{\star}=$-4.774\text{\,}\mathrm{eV}\text{\,}\mathrm{\SIUnitSymbolAngstrom}$,\quad v_{\star}^{\prime}=0,\quad M=0,\quad\lambda=0.5330\absolutevalue{\vec{a}_{M1}}

\displaystyle W_{1}=W_{2}=44.06\text{meV},\quad W_{3}=W_{4}={48.35}\text{meV},\quad J=23.75\text{meV},\quad K=5.11\text{meV},\quad U_{2}=3.709\text{meV}

(162)

The ground states we considered in
Fig.

9
are

\displaystyle|\Psi_{\nu=-3}\rangle=\prod_{\mathbf{k}}\hat{d}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},+,+,\uparrow}|\text{RB}\rangle,\quad\quad|\Psi_{\nu=-2}\rangle=\prod_{\mathbf{k}}\hat{d}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},+,+,\uparrow}\hat{d}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},-,+,\uparrow}|\text{RB}\rangle,

\displaystyle|\Psi_{\nu=-1}\rangle=\prod_{\mathbf{k}}\hat{d}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},+,+,\uparrow}\hat{d}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},-,+,\uparrow}\hat{d}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},+,-,\uparrow}|\text{RB}\rangle,\quad\quad|\Psi_{\nu=0}\rangle=\prod_{\mathbf{k}}\hat{d}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},+,+,\uparrow}\hat{d}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},-,+,\uparrow}\hat{d}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},+,-,\uparrow}\hat{d}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},-,-,\uparrow}|\text{RB}\rangle.

(163)

Figure 9:
Charge-(
\pm 1
) excitations at
w_{0}/w_{1}=0.5
with
\nu=-3
(a),
\nu=-2
(b),
\nu=-1
(c) and
\nu=0
(d).

Appendix C
Chern bands with localized Berry curvature

In this appendix, we discuss the heavy fermion description of a model with gapped Chern bands with concentrated Berry curvature. Our simple
sp
-orbital model consists of one
s
orbital and one
p_{z}
orbital both located at the
1a
Wyckoff position of the square lattice. We take the following non-interacting Hamiltonian featuring both inversion symmetry and time-reversal symmetry
[
63
]

\displaystyle H_{0}=\sum_{\mathbf{k},\sigma}\psi_{\mathbf{k},\sigma}^{\dagger}h^{\sigma}_{\mathbf{k}}\psi_{\mathbf{k},\sigma},\quad\mbox{with}\quad\psi_{\mathbf{k},\sigma}=\begin{bmatrix}\hat{f}_{\mathbf{k},\sigma}&\hat{c}_{\mathbf{k},\sigma}\end{bmatrix}^{T}

\displaystyle h_{\mathbf{k}}^{\sigma}=\begin{bmatrix}\epsilon_{s}&t_{sp}\left[\sigma\sin(k_y)-i\sin(k_x)\right]\\
t_{sp}\left[\sigma\sin(k_y)+i\sin(k_x)\right]&\epsilon_{p}-t_{p}\left[\cos(k_x)+\cos(k_y)\right]\end{bmatrix},

(164)

where
\hat{f}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},\sigma}
(
\hat{c}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},\sigma}
) creates a
s
(
p_{z}
)-orbital electron at momentum
\mathbf{k}
with spin
\sigma
.
\epsilon_{s},\epsilon_{p}
denote the onsite energy of the
s
and
p
orbitals, respectively. At the same time,
t_{sp}
is the hopping between two orbitals and
t_{p}
denotes the hopping of between the
p_{z}
orbitals. As a result of the time-reversal symmetry, the spin-
\uparrow
and spin-
\downarrow
bands are degenerate in this model.

We consider the situation where
|t_{p}|\gg|t_{sp}|
,
\epsilon_{p}=2t_{p}-\Delta\epsilon
with
|\Delta\epsilon|\ll|t_{p}|
, and
\epsilon_{s}=0
. The resulting band structure is shown in
Fig.

10
(a) for
\Delta\epsilon/t_{p}=-0.2
and
t_{sp}/t_{p}=0.1
and features a flat band and a dispersive band, both being doubly-degenerate.

We now discuss the band structure of the system. In the limit
t_{sp}\to 0
, the
p
orbitals develop a dispersive band with the band minimum at the
\Gamma
point and dispersion

\epsilon_{p,\mathbf{k}}=t_{p}\left[2-\cos(k_x)-\cos(k_y)\right]-\Delta\epsilon\approx-\Delta\epsilon+\frac{t_{p}\absolutevalue{\vk}^{2}}{2}

(165)

In this limit, the
s
orbitals form a perfectly flat trivial band. After introducing a non-zero
t_{sp}
, the atomic
s
orbitals and the dispersive
p
orbitals hybridize near the
\Gamma
point and a band inversion happens therein. Performing a leading-order expansion in
\mathbf{k}
of the
p
-orbital dispersion and of the
s
-
p
orbital hybridization, the single-particle Hamiltonian from
Eq.

164
can be approximated to

H_{0}\approx\sum_{|\mathbf{k}|<\Lambda_{c},\sigma}\left(-\Delta\epsilon+\frac{t_{p}|\mathbf{k}|^{2}}{2}\right)\hat{c}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},\sigma}\hat{c}_{\mathbf{k},\sigma}+\frac{t_{sp}}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{|\mathbf{k}|<\Lambda_{c},\mathbf{R},\sigma}\left[e^{-i\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{R}}(\sigma k_{y}+ik_{x})\hat{c}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},\sigma}\hat{f}_{\mathbf{R},\sigma}+\text{h.c.}\right]\,.

(166)

where
N
is the number of unit cells. We have also introduced a momentum cutoff
\Lambda_{c}
for the
c
electrons, since the
c
electron with large momenta are high-energy degrees of freedom and can be dropped. In practice, we can set the cutoff to the edge of the BZ.
From
Eq.

166
, it becomes evident that the system is equivalent to a heavy-fermion model, characterized by the hybridization between atomic
f
-electrons (
s
orbitals) and dispersive
c
electrons (
p
orbitals).

The eigenvalues of the single-particle Hamiltonian near the
\Gamma
point are

E^{\sigma}_{\mathbf{k},1}\approx-\Delta\epsilon+\frac{t_{p}\Delta\epsilon+2t_{sp}^{2}}{2\Delta\epsilon}\absolutevalue{\vk}^{2},\quad E_{\mathbf{k},2}\approx\frac{t_{sp}^{2}}{\Delta\epsilon}\absolutevalue{\vk}^{2},

(167)

with the corresponding eigenvectors being given by

\displaystyle U^{\sigma}_{\mathbf{k},1}\approx\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\sqrt{\frac{s_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}}{4}+t_{sp}^{2}\absolutevalue{\vk}^{2}}\left[\sqrt{\frac{s_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}}{4}+t_{sp}^{2}\absolutevalue{\vk}^{2}}-\frac{s_{\mathbf{k}}}{2}\right]}}\begin{bmatrix}\frac{s_{\mathbf{k}}}{2}-\sqrt{\frac{s_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}}{4}+t_{sp^{2}}\absolutevalue{\vk}^{2}}&t_{sp}\left(ik_{x}+\sigma k_{y}\right)\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle U^{\sigma}_{\mathbf{k},2}\approx\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\sqrt{\frac{s_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}}{4}+t_{sp}^{2}\absolutevalue{\vk}^{2}}\left[\sqrt{\frac{s_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}}{4}+t_{sp}^{2}\absolutevalue{\vk}^{2}}+\frac{s_{\mathbf{k}}}{2}\right]}}\begin{bmatrix}\frac{s_{\mathbf{k}}}{2}+\sqrt{\frac{s_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}}{4}+t_{sp^{2}}\absolutevalue{\vk}^{2}}&t_{sp}\left(ik_{x}+\sigma k_{y}\right)\end{bmatrix}

(168)

where
s_{\mathbf{k}}=-\Delta\epsilon+t_{p}|\mathbf{k}|^{2}/2
. It is easy to see that at the
\Gamma
point, the lowest bands are formed by the
p_{z}
orbital with
U_{\mathbf{k}=\mathbf{0},1}^{\sigma}=[0,1]
. As we move away
\Gamma
point, the lowest bands acquire more and more
s
-orbital weight. At large momenta
\absolutevalue{\vk}
, the
p_{z}
orbitals form high-energy bands, and the lowest-energy bands are mostly formed by the
s
orbitals.

We can also estimate the bandwidths of the bands. We first consider the lowest-energy bands. At the high-symmetry points, we have

E_{\mathbf{0},1}^{\sigma}=-\Delta\epsilon,\quad E_{(0,\pi),1}^{\sigma}=E_{(\pi,0),1}^{\sigma}\approx 0,\quad E_{(\pi,\pi),1}^{\sigma}\approx 0

(169)

where, away from
\Gamma
point, the lowest-energy bands are mostly formed by
s
orbitals with energy
\epsilon_{s}=0
. Therefore the bandwidth of the lowest-energy bands are
\sim|\Delta\epsilon|
. For the high-energy bands, the energies at high-symmetry points are

E_{\mathbf{0},2}^{\sigma}=0,\quad E_{(0,\pi),2}=E_{(\pi,0),2}\approx t_{p},\quad vE_{(\pi,\pi),2}\approx 2t_{p}

(170)

where away from
\Gamma
point, the high-energy bands are mostly formed by
p_{z}
orbitals with energy
-\Delta\epsilon+t_{p}[2-\cos(k_x)-\cos(k_y)]\approx t_{p}[2-\cos(k_x)-\cos(k_y)]
. Therefore, the high-energy bands have bandwidth
\sim 2|t_{p}|
. Thus, our current model comprises a two-fold degenerate narrow band with bandwidth
\sim|\Delta\epsilon|
and a two-fold degenerate dispersive band with bandwidth
\sim 2|t_{p}|
.

We now show that the two-fold degenerate low-energy narrow bands have Chern number
\pm 1
for spin-
\uparrow/\downarrow
. Since the system has inversion symmetry, it is sufficient to check the inversion eigenvalues of the lowest-energy band at high symmetry points
(0,0),(0,\pi),(\pi,0),(\pi,\pi)
. At the
\Gamma=(0,0)
point, the narrow bands are formed by
p_{z}
orbitals and have
-1
inversion eigenvalues. At the
(0,\pi),(\pi,0),(\pi,\pi)
points, the narrow bands are formed by
s
orbitals with
+1
inversion eigenvalue. Therefore, the two-fold degenerate narrow bands have Chern number
\pm 1
due to the band inversion at the
\Gamma
point.

Since the band inversion happens near the
\Gamma
point, we also analyze the Berry curvature near
\Gamma
point. We can calculate the Berry curvature of the lowest bands near
\Gamma
point using
Eq.

168
, which gives

\Omega(\mathbf{k})\approx\frac{8(2\Delta\epsilon+\absolutevalue{\vk}^{2}t_{p})t_{sp}^{2}}{\left[\left(-2\Delta\epsilon+t_{p}^{2}\absolutevalue{\vk}^{2}\right)^{2}+16\absolutevalue{\vk}^{2}t_{sp}^{2}\right]^{3/2}}

(171)

We can observe that, at large momentum the Berry curvature decays as
\frac{t_{sp}^{2}}{t_{p}^{2}\absolutevalue{\vk}}
. Since
|t_{sp}/t_{p}|\ll 1
, the Berry curvature quickly goes to zero at large momenta. In
Fig.

10
(b), we also show the numerically computed Berry curvature distribution, where we observe the Berry curvature concentrates near
\Gamma
point.

We can also introduce interactions to our current model. Here, we consider onsite Hubbard interactions for the
s
(
U
) and
p
(
V
) orbitals, as well as onsite repulsion between the two orbitals (
W
). The interaction term can be written as

\displaystyle H_{I}=

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{R}}\frac{U}{2}\left(\sum_{\sigma}:\hat{f}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{R},\sigma}\hat{f}_{\mathbf{R},\sigma}:\right)^{2}+\sum_{\mathbf{R},|\mathbf{k}|<\Lambda_{c},|\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q}|<\Lambda_{c}}\frac{We^{i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}}}{{N}}\left(\sum_{\sigma}:\hat{f}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{R},\sigma}\hat{f}_{\mathbf{R},\sigma}:\right)\left(\sum_{\sigma^{\prime}}:\hat{c}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},\sigma^{\prime}}\hat{c}_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q},\sigma^{\prime}}:\right)

\displaystyle+\sum_{|\mathbf{k}|<\Lambda_{c},|\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q}|<\Lambda_{c},|\mathbf{k^{\prime}}|<\Lambda_{c},|\mathbf{k^{\prime}}-\mathbf{q}|<\Lambda_{c}}\frac{V}{2N}\left(:\sum_{\sigma}\hat{c}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},\sigma}\hat{c}_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q},\sigma}:\right)\left(:\sum_{\sigma^{\prime}}\hat{c}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k^{\prime}},\sigma^{\prime}}\hat{c}_{\mathbf{k^{\prime}}-\mathbf{q},\sigma^{\prime}}:\right)

(172)

which takes a similar form to the interaction term of the THF model of TBG from
Eq.

66
.
For a given operator
\hat{O}
, we have also defined normal ordering as
:\hat{O}:=\hat{O}-\langle\Phi_{0}|\hat{O}|\Phi_{0}\rangle
with
|\Phi_{0}\rangle
the ground state of the non-interacting Hamiltonian. Finally, we mention that one could also consider the spinless case, where the only interaction term is the term proportional to
W
.

Figure 10:
Heavy-fermion model for Chern bands with localized Berry curvature. (a) shows the dispersion of the
sp
-orbital model defined in
Eq.

164
for
t_{p}=-1
,
t_{s}p=0.1
,
\epsilon_{s}=0
, and
\epsilon_{p}=1.8
. The band structure features a doubly-degenerate narrow Chern band (blue) and a doubly-degenerate dispersive band (orange). The dashed lines correspond to the band structure of the corresponding approximate Hamiltonian from
Eq.

166
. The berry curvature distributions of the narrow Chern band in arbitrary units is plotted in (b).

In summary, our current model yields two-fold degenerate narrow Chern bands near the Fermi energy, exhibiting concentrated Berry curvature near the
\Gamma
point. By including both the narrow Chern bands and the dispersive bands, the entire system can be effectively described by a heavy fermion model, as depicted by the single-particle Hamiltonian in
Eq.

164
(or
Eq.

166
). Here, the
s
orbitals correspond to localized
f
-electrons, while the
p_{z}
orbitals represent dispersive
c
-electrons. The flatness of the narrow bands arises from the localized nature of
s
orbitals (
f
-fermions), while the non-trivial topology emerges from the hybridization between
s
and
p_{z}
orbitals. Furthermore, after introducing interaction terms to the
s
and
p
orbitals, we could obtain a conventional interacting heavy fermion model. Thus we conclude that, in the case of flat Chern bands with concentrated Berry curvature, a heavy fermion description is still feasible.

Appendix D
Chiral-Symmetry-Protected Stable Anomaly

We now present the general discussion for chiral-symmetry-protected stable anomaly.
Given a generic 2D single-particle matrix Hamiltonian
h(\boldsymbol{k})
with chiral symmetry
\mathcal{C}
,
i.e.
,
\{\mathcal{C},h(\boldsymbol{k})\}=0
and
\mathcal{C}^{2}=1
.
Suppose

h(\boldsymbol{k}+\boldsymbol{G})=V_{\boldsymbol{G}}^{\dagger}h(\boldsymbol{k})V_{\boldsymbol{G}}

(173)

and

\left[V_{\boldsymbol{G}},\mathcal{C}\right]=0\ .

(174)

Let us focus on an isolated set of
2N
bands whose energies are symmetric with respect to the zero energy.
The eigenvectors (
U_{n,\boldsymbol{k}}
with
n=1,...,2N
) of the isolated set of bands are compatible with
\mathcal{C}
,
i.e.
, the eigenvectors of the isolated set of bands furnish a rep of
\mathcal{C}
:
\mathcal{C}U_{n,\boldsymbol{k}}=\sum_{n^{\prime}}U_{n^{\prime},\boldsymbol{k}}\left[D_{\mathcal{C}}(\boldsymbol{k})\right]_{n^{\prime}n}
with
D_{\mathcal{C}}(\boldsymbol{k})
unitary for any
n
and
\boldsymbol{k}
.
Equivalently, the projection matrix
P(\boldsymbol{k})
of the isolated set of 2N bands commutes with
\mathcal{C}
:

[\mathcal{C},P(\boldsymbol{k})]=0\ .

(175)

We can diagonalize
D_{\mathcal{C}}(\boldsymbol{k})
, which gives a “
+1
” subspace and a “
-1
” subspace, each of which has a dimension
N
.
Specifically, we denote

D_{\mathcal{C}}(\boldsymbol{k})v_{\pm,i,\boldsymbol{k}}=\pm v_{\pm,i,\boldsymbol{k}}\text{ for i=1,...,N}\ ,

(176)

and then we have

U_{\pm,i,\boldsymbol{k}}=\sum_{n}U_{n,\boldsymbol{k}}\left[v_{\pm,i,\boldsymbol{k}}\right]_{n}\ ,

(177)

where

\mathcal{C}U_{\pm,i,\boldsymbol{k}}=\pm U_{\pm,i,\boldsymbol{k}}\ .

(178)

The projection matrix of the “
\pm 1
” subspace of the isolated set of
2N
bands reads

P_{\pm}(\boldsymbol{k})=\sum_{i=1,...,N}U_{\pm,i,\boldsymbol{k}}U_{\pm,i,\boldsymbol{k}}^{\dagger}\ .

(179)

Owing to Eq. (
174
),
P_{\pm}(\boldsymbol{k}+\boldsymbol{G})=V_{\boldsymbol{G}}^{\dagger}P_{\pm}(\boldsymbol{k})V_{\boldsymbol{G}}
, which has the same behavior as
P(\boldsymbol{k})
.
Combined with the fact that they are projection matrices of the smooth
P(\boldsymbol{k})
for chiral eigenvalues
+
and
-
, respectively, both
P_{+}(\boldsymbol{k})
and
P_{-}(\boldsymbol{k})
are globally smooth in the momentum space.
Therefore, the Berry curvature of each subspace reads

F_{\pm}(\boldsymbol{k})=\text{Tr}\left[P_{+}(\boldsymbol{k})\left[\partial_{k_{x}}P_{+}(\boldsymbol{k}),\partial_{k_{y}}P_{+}(\boldsymbol{k})\right]\right]

(180)

which is smooth with
F_{\pm}(\boldsymbol{k}+\boldsymbol{G})=F_{\pm}(\boldsymbol{k})
, leading to well-defined Chern numbers for
P_{\pm}(\boldsymbol{k})
:

\text{Ch}_{\pm}=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int d^{2}k\ F_{\pm}(\boldsymbol{k})\ .

(181)

The total Chern number of the entire isolated set of
2N
bands is

\text{Ch}=\text{Ch}_{+}+\text{Ch}_{-}\ .

(182)

Note that Eq. (
174
) makes sure that
\text{Ch}_{\pm}
are well defined.
If Eq. (
174
) is violated by having
\{\mathcal{C},V(\boldsymbol{b}_{1})\}=0
with
\boldsymbol{b}_{1}
one primitive reciprocal lattice vector, we have
\mathcal{C}P_{+}(\boldsymbol{k}-\boldsymbol{b}_{1})=\mathcal{C}V_{\boldsymbol{b}_{1}}P_{+}(\boldsymbol{k})V_{\boldsymbol{b}_{1}}^{\dagger}=-P_{+}(\boldsymbol{k}-\boldsymbol{b}_{1})
and thus
F_{+}(\boldsymbol{k}+\boldsymbol{b}_{1})=F_{-}(\boldsymbol{k})
, which cannot guarantee well-defined
\text{Ch}_{\pm}
.

If
\text{Ch}\neq 0
, it is impossible to construct exponentially-localized Wannier functions for them, which is the classic obstruction from the total Chern number
[
283
]
.
Nevertheless, if
\text{Ch}_{\mathcal{C}}=\text{Ch}_{+}-\text{Ch}_{-}\neq 0
, it is also impossible to construct exponentially-localized Wannier functions for them while keeping the rep of
\mathcal{C}
strictly-onsite in the real space or momentum-independent in the momentum space, even if
\text{Ch}=0
.
To see this, let us assume we can rotate
U_{n,\boldsymbol{k}}
to a set of globally smooth vectors
\zeta_{a,\boldsymbol{k}}
with
a=1,2,...,2N
,
i.e.
,

\zeta_{a,\boldsymbol{k}}=\sum_{n=1}^{2N}U_{n,\boldsymbol{k}}R_{na}(\boldsymbol{k})

(183)

and all
\zeta_{a,\boldsymbol{k}}
are globally smooth while
V_{\boldsymbol{G}}^{\dagger}\zeta_{a,\boldsymbol{k}}=\zeta_{a,\boldsymbol{k}+\boldsymbol{G}}
.
We further assume the rep of
\mathcal{C}
furnished by
\zeta_{a,\boldsymbol{k}}
is independent of
\boldsymbol{k}
(which means that the rep of
\mathcal{C}
is strictly-onsite in the real space),
i.e.
,

\mathcal{C}\zeta_{a,\boldsymbol{k}}=\sum_{a^{\prime}}\zeta_{a^{\prime},\boldsymbol{k}}\left[\widetilde{D}_{\mathcal{C}}\right]_{a^{\prime}a}\ .

(184)

Then, we can diagonalize
\widetilde{D}_{\mathcal{C}}
:

\widetilde{D}_{\mathcal{C}}\widetilde{v}_{\pm,i}=\pm\widetilde{v}_{\pm,i}\text{ for }i=1,...,N\ ,

(185)

and have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\zeta_{\pm,i,\boldsymbol{k}}=\sum_{a}\zeta_{a,\boldsymbol{k}}\left[\widetilde{v}_{\pm,i}\right]_{a}\ .\end{split}

(186)

Since
\zeta_{\pm,i,\boldsymbol{k}}
are globally smooth and
V_{\boldsymbol{G}}^{\dagger}\zeta_{\pm,i,\boldsymbol{k}}=\zeta_{\pm,i,\boldsymbol{k}+\boldsymbol{G}}
, we have
\text{Ch}_{+}=\text{Ch}_{-}=0
.
As globally smooth
\zeta_{a,\boldsymbol{k}}
give exponentially localized Wannier functions, we know nonzero
\text{Ch}_{\mathcal{C}}=\text{Ch}_{+}-\text{Ch}_{-}\neq 0
forbids exponentially-localized Wannier functions with the rep of
\mathcal{C}
being strictly-onsite in the real space or momentum-independent in the momentum space, even if
\text{Ch}=0
.

Now we present an alternative way to evaluate
\text{Ch}_{+}-\text{Ch}_{-}
.
Suppose
h(\boldsymbol{k})
has several isolated band-touching points at zero energy, labeled as
\boldsymbol{k}_{l}
.
Then, for each node
\boldsymbol{k}_{l}
, we can define the chiral winding number
W_{l}
as

W_{l}=\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2\pi}\int_{\partial D_{l}}d\boldsymbol{k}\cdot\text{Tr}\left[Q^{-1}_{\boldsymbol{k}}\nabla_{\boldsymbol{k}}Q_{\boldsymbol{k}}\right]\ ,

(187)

where

\left[Q_{\boldsymbol{k}}\right]_{ii^{\prime}}=U_{+,i,\boldsymbol{k}}^{\dagger}h(\boldsymbol{k})U_{-,i^{\prime},\boldsymbol{k}}\ ,

(188)

D_{l}
is a disk-like region that only includes
\boldsymbol{k}_{l}
, and
\partial D_{l}
is the boundary (with direction) of
D_{l}
.
We now prove

\sum_{l}W_{l}=\text{Ch}_{\mathcal{C}}=\text{Ch}_{+}-\text{Ch}_{-}\ .

(189)

The proof is analugous to the proof for monopole Cooper pairing in Ref.
[
284
]
.
As the first step of the proof, we define

\displaystyle\begin{split}\boldsymbol{v}_{\boldsymbol{k}}&=\text{Tr}\left[\left(U_{-,\boldsymbol{k}}Q_{\boldsymbol{k}}^{-1}U_{+,\boldsymbol{k}}^{\dagger}\right)\mathrm{i}\nabla_{\boldsymbol{k}}\left(U_{+,\boldsymbol{k}}Q_{\boldsymbol{k}}U_{-,\boldsymbol{k}}^{\dagger}\right)\right]\\
&=\text{Tr}\left[U_{+,\boldsymbol{k}}^{\dagger}\mathrm{i}\nabla_{\boldsymbol{k}}U_{+,\boldsymbol{k}}\right]+\text{Tr}\left[Q_{\boldsymbol{k}}^{-1}\mathrm{i}\nabla_{\boldsymbol{k}}Q_{\boldsymbol{k}}\right]-\text{Tr}\left[U_{-,\boldsymbol{k}}^{\dagger}\mathrm{i}\nabla_{\boldsymbol{k}}U_{-,\boldsymbol{k}}\right]\\
&=\text{Tr}\left[Q_{\boldsymbol{k}}^{-1}\mathrm{i}\nabla_{\boldsymbol{k}}Q_{\boldsymbol{k}}\right]-\boldsymbol{A}_{+}(\boldsymbol{k})+\boldsymbol{A}_{-}(\boldsymbol{k})\ ,\end{split}

(190)

where

U_{\pm,\boldsymbol{k}}=\left(\begin{matrix}U_{\pm,1,\boldsymbol{k}}&U_{\pm,2,\boldsymbol{k}}&...&U_{\pm,N,\boldsymbol{k}}\end{matrix}\right)\ ,

(191)

and

\boldsymbol{A}_{\pm}(\boldsymbol{k})=-\mathrm{i}\text{Tr}\left[U_{\pm,\boldsymbol{k}}^{\dagger}\nabla_{\boldsymbol{k}}U_{\pm,\boldsymbol{k}}\right]\ .

(192)

\boldsymbol{v}_{\boldsymbol{k}}
is smooth except at
\boldsymbol{k}_{l}
’s, since both
U_{+,\boldsymbol{k}}^{\dagger}U_{+,\boldsymbol{k}}
and
U_{-,\boldsymbol{k}}^{\dagger}U_{-,\boldsymbol{k}}
are smooth, and
Q_{\boldsymbol{k}}^{-1}=U_{-,\boldsymbol{k}}^{\dagger}h^{-1}(\boldsymbol{k})U_{+,\boldsymbol{k}}
.
Moreover,
\boldsymbol{v}_{\boldsymbol{k}+\boldsymbol{G}}=\boldsymbol{v}_{\boldsymbol{k}}
.
The next step is to choose
D_{l}
such that (i) two
D_{l}
’s either have no intersection or intersect only on the boundaries, and (ii)
\cup_{l}D_{l}=1BZ
.
Then, we have

\frac{1}{2\pi}\sum_{l}\int_{\partial D_{l}}d\boldsymbol{k}\cdot\boldsymbol{v}_{\boldsymbol{k}}=0\ ,

(193)

since the integration always cancels along a line shared by two neighboring
D_{l}
’s.
Combined with Eq. (
190
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\frac{1}{2\pi}\sum_{l}\int_{\partial D_{l}}d\boldsymbol{k}\cdot\left[\text{Tr}\left[Q_{\boldsymbol{k}}^{-1}\mathrm{i}\nabla_{\boldsymbol{k}}Q_{\boldsymbol{k}}\right]-\boldsymbol{A}_{+}(\boldsymbol{k})+\boldsymbol{A}_{-}(\boldsymbol{k})\right]\\
&=\sum_{l}W_{l}-\frac{1}{2\pi}\sum_{l}\int_{\partial D_{l}}d\boldsymbol{k}\cdot\boldsymbol{A}_{+}(\boldsymbol{k})+\frac{1}{2\pi}\sum_{l}\int_{\partial D_{l}}d\boldsymbol{k}\cdot\boldsymbol{A}_{-}(\boldsymbol{k})\\
&=\sum_{l}W_{l}-\frac{1}{2\pi}\sum_{l}\int_{D_{l}}dk^{2}F_{+}(\boldsymbol{k})+\frac{1}{2\pi}\sum_{l}\int_{D_{l}}dk^{2}F_{-}(\boldsymbol{k})\\
&=\sum_{l}W_{l}-\text{Ch}_{+}+\text{Ch}_{-}=0\ ,\end{split}

(194)

leading to Eq. (
189
).
In the derivation, we have used
F_{\pm}(\boldsymbol{k})=\nabla_{\boldsymbol{k}}\times\boldsymbol{A}_{\pm}(\boldsymbol{k})
, and used the fact that we can always choose
U_{\pm,\boldsymbol{k}}
to be smooth within one single
D_{l}
(as it is disk-like) to use Stoke’s theorem for the second equality.
We note that
U_{\pm,\boldsymbol{k}}
cannot be made globally smooth if
\text{Ch}_{\pm}
are nonzero.

Based on Eq. (
189
), we know that as long as
\sum_{l}W_{l}
is nonzero, any isolated set of
2N
chiral-symmetric bands have chiral-protected Wannier obstruction, leading to a stable chiral-protected anomaly.

Appendix E
Twisted Checkerboard Model

This section contained more details on the twisted checkerboard model in Ref.
[
67
]
